4.2
Infinite tower of projected soft graviton theorems

We shall now revisit the argument presented in
[
48
]
to establish the direct connection between the infinite tower of the conformal Carrollian primaries
\left\{S^{+}_{k}\right\}
and the infinite hierarchy of soft graviton theorems obeyed by amplitudes at tree-level after certain projections
[
101
,
102
]
. In particular, we provide a symmetry-based justification of the crucial OPE formula (
4.12
) and extend the relation between the
u\to\infty
limit in the Carrollian CFT and the soft limit in the bulk discussed in Section
3
.

The OPE formulae for
S^{+}_{2}
with a conformal Carrollian primary operator
\Phi
, the corresponding Ward identities and the relation with the subsubleading soft graviton theorem are reviewed in Appendix
F
. Using a
\text{Witt}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\displaystyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\textstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[8.36108pt]{\raisebox{-0.83334pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[7.36115pt]{\raisebox{-0.83336pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}}Lw_{1+\infty}
symmetry argument, there we have also provided a partial derivation of the
S^{+}_{2}\Phi
OPE (
F.1
), originally postulated in
[
48
]
, hence providing a stronger case for the said OPE formula. As outlined towards the end of Appendix
F
, beginning with the
S^{+}_{2}\Phi
OPE (
F.1
) one can then recursively deduce the
S^{+}_{k\geq 3}\Phi
OPEs for a conformal Carrollian primary
\Phi
with dimensions
(h,\bar{h})
and
\left(\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot\Phi\right)=0=\left({{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot\Phi\right)
as below:

S_{k}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\\
\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\sum_{r=0}^{k}\frac{\left(u-u_{p}\right)^{k-r}}{(k-r)!}\sum_{m=0}^{r}\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m+1}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\frac{(-2\bar{h})_{r-m}}{(r-m)!\cdot m!}\text{ }\bar{\partial}^{m}_{p}\Phi_{r-1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})

(4.12)

where the unique local ascendant fields
\left\{\Phi_{r}\right\}
satisfy (in the OPE limit):

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{r}\Phi_{r}\sim\Phi\text{ ($r\geq 1$)}\hskip 14.22636pt,\hskip 14.22636pt\Phi_{0}=\Phi\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Phi_{-1}=\dot{\Phi}\,.

(4.13)

It is reassuring to note that, besides the symmetry-derived
S^{+}_{k}S^{+}_{l}
OPEs (
4.7
), there exist other examples, found in
[
47
]
by explicitly analysing the bulk Einstein-Yang-Mills theory, consistent with the above
S^{+}_{k}\Phi
OPE (
4.12
). To see this, we first apply the decomposition (
4.5
) to extract the
S^{+}_{1-k(e)}\Phi
part from the
S^{+}_{1-k}\Phi
OPE and then impose the
u\rightarrow\infty
limit so that
\frac{1}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}
becomes
\frac{1}{z-z_{p}}
. Substituting appropriate values for
\bar{h}
next, one finally recovers the OPEs in
[
47
]
of the (celestial) soft graviton operators with the Carrollian graviton and gluon operators. This observation is again consistent with the expectation that the 2D celestial conformally soft graviton primary
H^{k}(z,\bar{z})
is nothing but the object
-iS^{+}_{1-k(e)}(\infty,z,\bar{z})
. We will come back to this identification in Section
4.4
. More importantly, we have also obtained the OPE (
4.12
) at finite
u
directly from the Einstein-Yang-Mills collinear splitting (
4.26
) in Appendix
G.2
.

We now discuss how the infinite number of ‘projected’ soft graviton theorems
[
101
,
102
]
can be encoded into the Ward identities of the infinite tower of conformal Carrollian primaries
S^{+}_{k}
. We note that the
S_{k\geq 3}^{+}
Ward identity below can not be uniquely fixed (even up to temporal contact terms) by demanding finiteness (at
z\neq z_{p}
) of the
\langle S_{k\geq 3}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
correlators; they are rather determined
11
11

11

For a primary singlet
\Phi
with holomorphic weight
h
, its correlators satisfy:
\lim\limits_{z\rightarrow\infty}z^{2h}\left\langle\Phi(u,z,\bar{z})X\right\rangle=\text{finite}
, just as in a 2D CFT. Since the field
S^{+}_{k}
has
h=\frac{3-k}{2}
, the correlators
\left\langle S^{+}_{k\geq 3}(u,z,\bar{z})X\right\rangle
grow polynomially as
z^{k-3}
near
z\rightarrow\infty
. These polynomials can not be fixed from symmetry alone. In contrast, the
\left\langle S^{+}_{k\leq 2}(u,z,\bar{z})X\right\rangle
correlators vanish at
z\rightarrow\infty
.
up to a
(k-3)
-th degree polynomial in
z
:

i\langle S_{k}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle\\
=\sum_{p=1}^{n}\theta(u-u_{p})\sum_{r=0}^{k}\frac{\left(u-u_{p}\right)^{k-r}}{(k-r)!}\sum_{m=0}^{r}\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m+1}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{(-2\bar{h}_{p})_{r-m}}{(r-m)!\cdot m!}\text{ }\bar{\partial}^{m}_{p}\partial_{u_{p}}^{1-r}\langle X\rangle\,.

(4.14)

A very similar situation arises for the 4D graviton amplitudes. The (energetically) soft graviton factorization extends beyond the subsubleading order only for a certain projected part of the amplitude; such a projection leaves out a homogeneous piece that can not be fixed by the Ward identities of the large diffeomorphisms
[
101
]
or by gauge invariance
[
102
]
. It is then plausible that this homogeneous piece of the
\text{(sub)}^{k}
-leading soft graviton amplitude corresponds to the undetermined polynomial-in-
z
part of the
\langle S_{k(e)}^{+}(\infty,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
‘correlator’ for
k\geq 3
with both vanishing for
k\leq 2
.

In any case, similarly to what was found in Section
3
for the cases
k=0,1
, the
\langle S_{k(e)}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
part of the above
S^{+}_{k}
Ward identity is equivalent to the projected part of the
\text{(sub)}^{k}
-leading energetically soft graviton theorem in the
u\rightarrow\infty
limit when all the primaries in
X
possess
\Delta=1
and
\left(\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot\Phi\right)=0=\left({{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot\Phi\right)
. This can be directly inferred from the observation that, by temporal-Fourier transforming the ‘partial’
S^{+}_{k}
Ward identity (
4.14
) and then explicitly making the energy of the field
S^{+}_{k}
soft and substituting all
\Delta_{p}=1
, one obtains the ‘shifted’ soft graviton expansion of the projected amplitude
[
48
]
:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\left\langle\tilde{S}_{k}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle=\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i^{k-1}\left[\sum_{r=0}^{k}\frac{F^{(r)}}{(\epsilon\omega+i\delta)^{k+1-r}}+\mathcal{O}(\omega^{0})\right]\left\langle\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle

(4.15)

where
\tilde{S}^{+}_{k}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})
is the temporal-Fourier transformation of the field
S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})
,

\displaystyle\tilde{S}^{+}_{k}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})=\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}du\text{ }e^{i\epsilon\omega u}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{(with $\omega>0$)}\,,

(4.16)

similar for the operators in
\tilde{X}
and we have used the fact that:

\displaystyle\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}du\text{ }e^{i\epsilon\omega u}\text{ }\theta\left(u-u_{p}\right)\frac{\left(u-u_{p}\right)^{n}}{n!}=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{e^{i\epsilon\omega u_{p}}}{\left(-i\epsilon\omega+\delta\right)^{n+1}}\,.

(4.17)

The
i\delta
-prescription above bears the imprint of the retarded initial conditions, e.g. (
3.11
). In (
4.15
),
F^{(0)}
is the Weinberg leading soft factor
[
17
,
16
]
,
F^{(1)}
is the Cachazo-Strominger subleading soft factor
[
12
,
13
]
and
F^{(2)}
is the subsubleading soft factor
[
12
,
103
]
. The quantity
F^{(k)}
has a
(k+1)
-th degree polynomial in
(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})
in the numerator, and
(z-z_{p})
in the denominator and depends on
\{\omega_{p}\}
and
\{s_{p}\}
. The terms at
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{0})
and beyond can not be completely
determined from the Ward identity (
4.14
) alone for finite
k
, because of the finite contributions coming from the yet-unknown temporal contact terms. On the other hand, the

\omega\rightarrow 0
Fourier counterpart of the relation (
4.3
) which is exact at
u\rightarrow\infty
:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}(-i\epsilon\omega+\delta)\left\langle\tilde{S}_{k+1}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle=\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\left\langle\tilde{S}_{k}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle

(4.18)

implies that the
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{0})
term of the soft factor in (
4.15
) must be
F^{(k+1)}
and, by induction, the
\mathcal{O}(\omega^{p})
term,
F^{(k+1+p)}
with
p\geq 0
. Thus, multiplying both sides of (
4.15
) with
(\epsilon\omega)^{k}
, recognizing that:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}(-i\epsilon\omega+\delta)^{k}\left\langle\tilde{S}_{k}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle=\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\left\langle\tilde{S}_{0}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle

and finally setting
k\rightarrow\infty
, we recover the complete standard soft graviton expansion of the projected amplitudes
[
102
]
in 4D flat space as an insertion of the soft operator
\tilde{S}_{0}^{+}(\epsilon\omega\rightarrow 0,z,\bar{z})
:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\left\langle\tilde{S}_{0}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\left[\frac{F^{(0)}}{\epsilon\omega+i\delta}+\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}{F^{(k+1)}}{(\epsilon\omega+i\delta)^{k}}\right]\left\langle\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle\,.

(4.19)

That we needed to explicitly take the energetically soft limit for the
i\tilde{S}_{0}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})
field to obtain the above soft graviton expansion, suggests that the insertion of the 3D conformal Carrollian field
iS^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})
in a correlator may give us interesting information on graviton scattering amplitudes in flat space at finite energy. The central goal of the present work is to establish that this is indeed the case.

4.3
Collinear limit of scattering amplitudes

As we have reviewed, the
\langle S_{k(e)}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
part of the
\langle S_{k}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
Ward identity (
4.14
) provides, exclusively in the
u\rightarrow\infty
limit, a 3D Carrollian holographic description of the tree-level projected
(\text{sub})^{k}
-leading soft graviton theorem in 4D. We now wish to uncover the 4D bulk physics at non-zero finite
\omega
that is encoded by these Ward identities or the corresponding OPEs (
4.12
) when
u
is kept finite.

Clearly, the yet unknown temporal contact terms in the Ward identities (
4.14
) will produce, upon temporal Fourier transformation, non-zero contributions at generic
\omega
. So, we have to first determine these temporal contact terms. For this purpose, taking cue from the above-described derivation of the tree-level projected soft graviton expansion (
4.19
), we demand that the following relation holds exactly (for all
k\geq 0
):

\displaystyle\partial_{u}S^{+}_{k+1}(u,z,\bar{z})=S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})

(4.20)

by extending the validity of (
4.3
) which was only an OPE-level statement (i.e. valid up to temporal contact terms). Hence, the primaries
S_{k}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})
correspond to an infinite tower of
\partial_{u}
-ascendants of
S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})
, as defined in (
2.8
). Meanwhile,
\partial_{u}S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})
must still vanish up to temporal contact terms as before. The main motivation behind demanding (
4.20
) is to extend the soft-relation (
4.18
) to any non-zero
\omega
. Similarly, the OPE-level relations (
4.13
) are also extended into the following exact conditions:

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{r}\Phi_{r}=\Phi\text{ ($r\geq 1$)}\hskip 14.22636pt,\hskip 14.22636pt\Phi_{0}=\Phi\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Phi_{-1}=\dot{\Phi}\,.

(4.21)

By virtue of the conditions (
4.20
), the temporal contact terms in an
\langle S_{k}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
correlator are all related to those in any other
\langle S_{l}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
.

With the exact constraints (
4.20
) in possession, we are now ready to take temporal Fourier transformation of the
\langle S_{\{k\}}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
Ward identities. But, since the
\langle S_{k}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
Ward identity (up to temporal contact terms) for
k\geq 3
can be fixed by symmetry arguments alone only up to a
(k-3)
-th degree polynomial in
z
, it will be judicial to temporal Fourier transform the corresponding OPE (
4.12
) instead, by keeping track of the temporal contact terms, to reliably recover the complete physics of the 4D massless scattering amplitudes at the leading-order in the collinear singularity as that undetermined polynomial is clearly subleading in the OPE/collinear limit. Restoring the temporal step-function in the OPE (
4.12
), keeping in mind that there are yet unknown temporal contact terms, performing the temporal Fourier transformation (
4.16
) and finally using (
4.17
), one obtains (with
\omega,\omega_{p}\geq 0
):

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{k}^{+}

\displaystyle(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{\Phi}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

(4.22)

\displaystyle\sim\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}

\displaystyle-i(-i\epsilon\omega+\delta)^{-k}\text{ }\sum_{r=0}^{k}\left(\frac{\epsilon\omega+i\delta}{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega}\right)^{r-1}\sum_{m=0}^{r}\Big[\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m+1}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{(-2\bar{h}_{p})_{r-m}}{(r-m)!\cdot m!}

\displaystyle\times\text{ }\bar{\partial}^{m}_{p}\tilde{\Phi}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega,z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\Big]+f_{k}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z};\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega,z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

where the function
f_{k}
collects the contributions from the temporal contact terms and
\sim
denotes ‘modulo terms regular in the holomorphic collinear limit’. It is noteworthy that the shift in energy
\omega_{p}
of the field
\tilde{\Phi}
by the amount
\omega
has its origin in the temporal step-function hidden in the
j\epsilon
-prescription of the OPE (
4.12
).

Iterating the relation (
4.20
)
l
-times, it is readily observed that the coefficient of
\frac{(u-u_{p})^{n}}{n!}
with
0\leq n\leq l
in the
S^{+}_{k+l+1}\Phi
OPE is the same as that of the
\partial_{u}^{l-n}\delta(u-u_{p})
temporal contact term in the
S^{+}_{k}\Phi
OPE. Moreover, the
S^{+}_{k}\Phi
OPE (
4.12
) is a
k
-th degree polynomial in
(u-u_{p})
. Thus, we expect the
S^{+}_{k=\infty}\Phi
OPE to generate the temporal contact terms in the
S^{+}_{l}\Phi
OPEs for finite
l
via the iteration of (
4.20
), while containing none itself. This amounts to setting
f_{k=\infty}=0
identically in the momentum space that, from (
4.22
), leads to:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\left[(-i\epsilon\omega+\delta)^{k}\tilde{S}_{k}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\right]_{k=\infty}\tilde{\Phi}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

(4.23)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\epsilon\omega+i\delta}{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega}\right)^{r-1}\sum_{m=0}^{r}\left[\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m+1}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{(-2\bar{h}_{p})_{r-m}}{(r-m)!\cdot m!}\times\text{ }\bar{\partial}^{m}_{p}\tilde{\Phi}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega,z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\right]\,.

Finally, remembering that:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}

\displaystyle(-i\epsilon\omega+\delta)^{k}\tilde{S}_{k}^{+}(\epsilon\omega)=\tilde{S}_{0}^{+}(\epsilon\omega)

(4.24)

\displaystyle(-i\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p})^{r}\tilde{\Phi}_{r}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p})=\tilde{\Phi}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p})

where the first line is now valid for any
\omega\geq 0
,
k\geq 0
as a consequence of the exact relation (
4.20
) with the retarded initial condition, while the second one for any
\omega_{p}\geq 0
,
r\geq-1
due to the relations (
4.21
) with the temporal boundary condition unspecified, and performing the summation,
12
12

12

Though this summation converges only when
\left|\frac{\epsilon\omega}{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega}\right|<1
, one can analytically continue to any
\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}\neq-\epsilon\omega
.
we find:

\displaystyle i\tilde{S}_{0}^{+}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{\Phi}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

(4.25)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}-i\delta}{\epsilon\omega+i\delta}\left(\frac{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega}{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}-i\delta}\right)^{{s}_{p}}\text{ }\tilde{\Phi}\left(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega,z_{p},\frac{\left(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}-i\delta\right)\bar{z}_{p}+(\epsilon\omega+i\delta)\bar{z}}{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega}\right)

after substituting
\Delta_{p}=1
since we want the conformal Carrollian primary
\Phi
to correspond to a 4D massless scattering field with ‘outgoing’ helicity
s_{p}
.

Intriguingly, the RHS above is recognised to be displaying the leading order universal holomorphic collinear splitting property involving at least one positive helicity graviton
h_{+2}
, of the 4D Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitudes
[
104
,
20
]
. More precisely, the ‘OPE’ (
4.25
) formally resembles the following collinear splitting (in the limit
{z\approx z_{p}}
and
\omega,\omega_{p}>0
):

\displaystyle{\Phi}^{*}_{s_{p}}\left(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega,z_{p},\frac{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}+\epsilon\omega\bar{z}}{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}+\epsilon\omega}\right)\longrightarrow h_{+2}(\epsilon\omega,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}_{s_{p}}(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

(4.26)

where the energies and helicities are expressed in the ‘all outgoing’ convention and
\Phi^{*}
denotes a virtual particle. This observation provides yet another strong evidence in support of the direct correspondence between the conformal Carrollian primary
S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})
and the 4D positive helicity (hard) graviton
h_{+2}
. The explicit correspondence was introduced in Section
3.5
and will be further discussed in Section
4.4
for all
S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})
. The
S^{+}_{k}\Phi
OPEs (
4.12
) at finite
u
thus collectively encode the aforementioned collinear splitting property of the 4D bulk Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitudes.

It is worth emphasizing that we have reached the bulk null momentum space holomorphic collinear splitting property (
4.25
) by temporal Fourier transforming the
S^{+}_{k=\infty}\Phi
OPE that was itself obtained from purely Carrollian physics, without any hint from the bulk theory. We only needed to demand that a field
S^{+}_{2}
obeying the relation (
4.20
)
k=1
exists in the Carrollian CFT and that the two assumptions stated in Appendix
E
hold. Using only the Carrollian symmetry arguments under those two assumptions, the general
S^{+}_{k}S^{+}_{l}
OPE (
4.7
) and the corresponding symmetry algebra
Lw_{1+\infty}
were derived first and then, using that algebra, all the
S^{+}_{k}\Phi
OPEs (
4.12
) could be inferred (as explained in Appendix
F
).

In celestial holography, the above sequence was derived in quite the opposite way. The bulk collinear splitting (
4.26
) is itself the starting point (usually) for deriving, via Mellin transformations, the general OPE involving at least one celestial conformal primary graviton
[
20
]
from which the 2D celestial conformally soft graviton OPE
[
22
]
, that contains the
Lw_{1+\infty}
symmetry
[
23
]
, is obtained. Though one can utilize the constraints arising from the 2D celestial subsubleading conformally soft graviton theorem to derive the former OPE
[
20
]
, include contributions from all the anti-holomorphic descendants
[
22
]
and finally perform the inverse Mellin transformations to obtain the bulk collinear splitting (
4.25
), the first step itself explicitly requires some inputs from the bulk Einstein-Yang-Mills theory,
13
13

13

However in
[
105
]
, working in a light-transformed basis, the leading gluon OPE singularity along with the coefficients was uniquely fixed using only symmetry arguments, without any input from the bulk.
unlike the purely holographic Carrollian derivation described above.

4.4
Carrollian CFT currents at
\mathscr{I}

We now extend the discussion presented in Section
3.5
to express the whole tower of Carrollian CFT currents
S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})
in terms of bulk radiative modes (
3.39
)-(
3.40
). First, the relation (
4.20
) can be inverted as

\displaystyle S^{+}_{k+1}(u,z,\bar{z})=\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }S^{+}_{k}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\,.

(4.27)

Starting from the proposal (
3.43
) for the field
S^{+}_{0}
, and using the above formula iteratively, we can find the following relations between the fields
S^{+}_{k\geq 0}
and the asymptotic shear:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

(4.28)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{k}\frac{u^{k-r}}{(k-r)!}\cdot\frac{(-)^{r}}{r!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }{u^{\prime}}^{r}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]\,.

Comparing the second line above with the decomposition (
4.5
), we infer that (with
S^{+}_{0(e)}\equiv S^{+}_{0}
):

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}S^{+}_{r(e)}(u,z,\bar{z})=\frac{(-)^{r}}{r!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }{u^{\prime}}^{r}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]\,.

(4.29)

As suggested above, we can identify the celestial conformally soft graviton primary
H^{1-k}(z,\bar{z})
with the object
-iS^{+}_{k(e)}(\infty,z,\bar{z})
. This leads to the following well-known expression of the former in terms of the shear
[
24
]
:

\displaystyle H^{1-k}(z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle\equiv-iS^{+}_{k(e)}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi i(-)^{k}}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}du^{\prime}\text{ }{u^{\prime}}^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]\,.

(4.30)

It was shown in
[
48
]
that all the fields
S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})
are 3D conformal Carrollian primaries of the form (
2.7
) i.e. singlets. In view of the relation (
4.28
), this should also follow from the transformation properties of the asymptotic shear under the bulk supertranslations and superrotations if the proposed correspondence (
3.43
) is to be valid. We check this explicitly. Under the supertranslation with parameter
\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})
, the variation of the shear is given by (see e.g.
[
35
]
):

\begin{split}&\delta_{\mathcal{T}}C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})=\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\partial_{u}C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})-2\partial_{z}^{2}\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\,,\\
&\delta_{\mathcal{T}}D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})=\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\partial_{u}D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})+2\partial_{z}^{2}\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\,,\end{split}

(4.31)

with the antipodal matching condition
[
13
,
6
]
already taken care of. Accordingly, the variation of the field
S^{+}_{k}
(
4.28
) is found to be:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}\delta_{\mathcal{T}}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[\delta_{\mathcal{T}}C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+\delta_{\mathcal{T}}D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\cdot\frac{1}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}^{2}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})S^{+}_{k-1}(u,z,\bar{z})

(4.32)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\partial_{u}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})

where the last line follows from (
4.20
). This is the correct transformation law of a conformal Carrollian primary singlet under supertranslation. To go from the second line to the third line, we have assumed that
14
14

14

Schwartzian falloffs in
u
are usually assumed in the phase space approach of
Lw_{1+\infty}
, see e.g.
[
24
,
106
,
25
,
31
]
.

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}u^{k}\partial_{u}\left[C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})\right]=0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for any $k\geq 0$}\,.

(4.33)

Thus, even though the shear individually transforms inhomogeneously as in (
4.31
) under supertranslation, the fields
S^{+}_{k}
all have homogeneous transformation properties since the inhomogeneous pieces cancel each other due to the combination (
4.28
).

It is worth mentioning that none of the objects
S^{+}_{k(e)}(u,z,\bar{z})
(
4.29
) for
k\geq 1
transforms as a conformal Carrollian primary under the supertranslations since

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}}S^{+}_{k(e)}(u,z,\bar{z})=\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\left[\partial_{u}S^{+}_{k(e)}+S^{+}_{k-1(e)}\right](u,z,\bar{z})\,.

(4.34)

The
u\to\infty
limit of these transformations is however consistent with results previously found in the literature for the subleading soft graviton, see e.g.
[
107
,
108
]
.

Let us now consider a holomorphic superrotation with parameter
\mathcal{Y}(z)
, under which the variation of the shear is
[
35
]

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Y}}C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})=\left[\partial\mathcal{Y}(z)\left(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}\right)+\mathcal{Y}(z)\partial\right]C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})-u\partial^{3}\mathcal{Y}(z)\,,

(4.35)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Y}}D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})=\left[\partial\mathcal{Y}(z)\left(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}\right)+\mathcal{Y}(z)\partial\right]D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})+u\partial^{3}\mathcal{Y}(z)\,,

with the anti-podal matching condition
[
13
,
6
]
already implemented. The variation of the field
S^{+}_{k}
is then obtained as

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2\pi}\delta_{\mathcal{Y}}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[\delta_{\mathcal{Y}}C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+\delta_{\mathcal{Y}}D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[\left\{\partial\mathcal{Y}(z)\left(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{u^{\prime}}{2}\partial_{u^{\prime}}\right)+\mathcal{Y}(z)\partial\right\}C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right.

\displaystyle\left.-u^{\prime}\partial^{3}\mathcal{Y}(z)+\left\{\partial\mathcal{Y}(z)\left(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{u^{\prime}}{2}\partial_{u^{\prime}}\right)+\mathcal{Y}(z)\partial\right\}D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+u^{\prime}\partial^{3}\mathcal{Y}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2\pi}\mathcal{Y}(z)\partial S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})+2\cdot\frac{-1}{2\pi}\partial\mathcal{Y}(z)S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle+\partial\mathcal{Y}(z)\cdot\frac{1}{2\cdot k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }u^{\prime}(u-u^{\prime})^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}^{2}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2\pi}\mathcal{Y}(z)\partial S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})+\partial\mathcal{Y}(z)\left(\frac{3-k}{2}+\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}\right)\frac{-1}{2\pi}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})\,,

(4.36)

exactly as required for a conformal Carrollian primary singlet
S^{+}_{k}
with holomorphic weight
h=\frac{3-k}{2}
, see (
2.7
). Here, to reach the last line from the third one, we have used the assumption (
4.33
) and the relation (
4.20
). Again, the inhomogeneous terms in the variations of the individual shears get mutually cancelled inside the combination (
4.28
), leaving the variations of the fields
S^{+}_{k}
under the superrotations homogeneous. In a similar way, from the variation of the shear under the anti-holomorphic superrotations, we can also verify that the field
S^{+}_{k}
(
4.28
) transforms as a conformal Carrollian primary with
\bar{h}=-\frac{1+k}{2}
.

We may also consider the following variations of the shears under the Carrollian diffeomorphisms (
3.33
) expressed as
\zeta=\bar{V}(z,\bar{z})\bar{\partial}+\frac{u}{2}\bar{\partial}\bar{V}(z,\bar{z})\partial_{u}

[
89
,
90
]
(with
C_{z\bar{z}}=0
initially):

\displaystyle\delta_{\bar{V}}C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})=\left[\bar{\partial}\bar{V}\left(\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}-\frac{1}{2}\right)+\bar{V}(z,\bar{z})\bar{\partial}\right]C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})-u\partial^{2}\bar{\partial}\bar{V}\,,

(4.37)

\displaystyle\delta_{\bar{V}}D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})=\left[\bar{\partial}\bar{V}\left(\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}-\frac{1}{2}\right)+\bar{V}(z,\bar{z})\bar{\partial}\right]D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})+u\partial^{2}\bar{\partial}\bar{V}

to find, upon comparison with (
A.30
), that the fields
S^{+}_{k}
(
4.28
) transform as primary singlets with
\bar{h}=-\frac{1+k}{2}
under the diffeomorphisms (
3.33
):

\displaystyle\delta_{\bar{V}}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})=\bar{V}(z,\bar{z})\bar{\partial}S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})+\bar{\partial}\bar{V}(z,\bar{z})\left(\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}-\frac{1+k}{2}\right)S^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})

(4.38)

which confirms that
\left\{S^{+}_{k}\right\}
are primary fields also under the
\text{Witt}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\displaystyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\textstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[8.36108pt]{\raisebox{-0.83334pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[7.36115pt]{\raisebox{-0.83336pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}}(\overline{\mathfrak{sl}_{2}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\displaystyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\textstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[8.36108pt]{\raisebox{-0.83334pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[7.36115pt]{\raisebox{-0.83336pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}}{\mathfrak{s}}_{\wedge})
symmetry. (See Appendix
C
for the defining conditions of a primary multiplet under the said symmetry.)

Specifying the above discussion to
k=0,1
and remembering that
\partial_{u}S^{+}_{0}\sim 0
inside an OPE (
B.5
), one can verify explicitly that the components of the Carrollian CFT stress tensor identified in (
3.44
) and (
3.48
) transform as a conformal Carrollian primary multiplet (see discussion around Equation (
2.9
)) under the algebra (
3.38
), i.e.

\begin{split}\delta_{(\mathcal{T}_{\wedge},\mathcal{Y},\bar{{V}})}{T^{u}}_{u}=&\Big[\mathcal{Y}\partial+\bar{V}\bar{\partial}+\frac{3}{2}\partial\mathcal{Y}+\frac{3}{2}\bar{\partial}\bar{V}\Big]{T^{u}}_{u}\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\text{since }\partial_{u}{T^{u}}_{u}\sim 0\right)\,,\\
\delta_{(\mathcal{T}_{\wedge},\mathcal{Y},\bar{V})}{T^{u}}_{\bar{z}}=&\Big[\Big(\mathcal{T}_{\wedge}+\frac{u}{2}(\partial\mathcal{Y}+\bar{\partial}\bar{V})\Big)\partial_{u}+\mathcal{Y}\partial+\bar{V}\bar{\partial}+\partial\mathcal{Y}+2\bar{\partial}\bar{V}\Big]{T^{u}}_{\bar{z}}\\
&+\frac{3}{2}\bar{\partial}\Big(\mathcal{T}_{\wedge}+\frac{u}{2}\bar{\partial}\bar{V}\Big){T^{u}}_{u}\,,\end{split}

(4.39)

where
\mathcal{T}_{\wedge}
and
\bar{V}
satisfy the following ‘wedge conditions’:
\bar{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\wedge}=0=\bar{\partial}^{3}\bar{V}
. However, under a supertranslation
\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})
not obeying the above wedge condition and an anti-holomorphic superrotation
\bar{\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{z})
, the components
{T^{u}}_{u}
and
{T^{u}}_{\bar{z}}
do not transform as primaries.

Similarly, the component
{T^{u}}_{z}
given by (
3.49
) does not transform as a primary under the general supertranslations
\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})
and the holomorphic superrotations
\mathcal{Y}(z)
. But, under the wedge supertranslations
\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\wedge}(z,\bar{z})
obeying
\partial^{2}\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\wedge}=0
, diffeomorphisms
V(z,\bar{z})
conjugate to (
3.33
) and satisfying
\partial^{3}V=0
, and arbitrary anti-holomorphic superrotations
\bar{\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{z})
, it transforms as a primary multiplet:

\displaystyle\delta_{(\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\wedge},V,\bar{\mathcal{Y}})}{T^{u}}_{z}=

\displaystyle\Big[\Big(\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\wedge}+\frac{u}{2}(\partial V+\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{Y}})\Big)\partial_{u}+V\partial+\bar{\mathcal{Y}}\bar{\partial}+2\partial V+\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{Y}}\Big]{T^{u}}_{z}

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\partial\Big(\bar{\mathcal{T}}_{\wedge}+\frac{u}{2}\partial V\Big){{\bar{T}}^{u}}_{\hskip 5.69054ptu}\,.

(4.40)

Importantly, the intersection of all the different kinds of transformations discussed above consists of precisely the ten global conformal Carrollian transformations. Thus, the stress-tensor components (
3.44
) (and its conjugate), (
3.48
), and (
3.49
) form a conformal Carrollian quasi-primary multiplet with scaling dimension
\Delta=3
and spin-boost matrices given by (
2.9
).

4.5
Carrollian CFT currents in momentum space

To make the relation with 4D scattering amplitudes more explicit, it is useful to present the expressions of
S_{k}^{+}
in momentum space, in terms of creation and annihilation operators. Let us start with
S^{+}_{0}
. Adapting the mode decomposition (
3.39
)-(
3.40
) to the retarded initial condition (
3.11
), inserting those into (
3.43
), and subtracting the non-Fourier soft contributions at
u=-\infty
,
15
15

15

The subtractions of
C_{zz}(-\infty,z,\bar{z})
and
D_{zz}(-\infty,z,\bar{z})
in (
3.43
) cancel the non-Fourier soft contributions, thus allowing for a well-defined Fourier-mode expansion; see e.g.
[
50
,
83
]
for related discussions.
we find

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}

\displaystyle\left[e^{-i\omega u}\left\{a_{+}^{\text{out}}-{a_{+}^{\text{in}}}\right\}(\omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad-\text{ }e^{i\omega u}\left\{{a_{-}^{\text{out}}}^{\dagger}-{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}\right\}(\omega-i\delta,z,\bar{z})\right]\,.

(4.41)

The temporal-Fourier transformation of the field
S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})
defined in (
4.16
) is then found to be (suppressing the
(z,\bar{z})
dependence):

\displaystyle\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\omega>0)=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\left[a_{+}^{\text{out}}-{a_{+}^{\text{in}}}\right](\omega+i\delta)\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317pt\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\omega<0)=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\left[{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}-{a_{-}^{\text{out}}}^{\dagger}\right](-\omega-i\delta)\,.

(4.42)

Now, starting from (
3.43
)
u→∞
in position space at
\mathscr{I}
, then using the mode-expansions (
3.39
)-(
3.40
), and the following delta-function convention:

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}d\omega\text{ }\delta(\omega)f(\omega)=\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\text{ }\delta(\omega)\theta(\omega)f(\omega)=\frac{1}{2}f(0^{+})\,,

one can obtain

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\omega}{2}\left[\left\{a_{+}^{\text{out}}-a_{+}^{\text{in}}\right\}(\omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})-\left\{{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}-{a_{-}^{\text{out}}}^{\dagger}\right\}(\omega-i\delta,z,\bar{z})\right]

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\omega}{2}\left[a_{+}^{\text{out}}(\omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})-{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}(\omega-i\delta,z,\bar{z})\right]

(4.43)

where the last equality holds inside of the correlators (compare with (
3.7
)). The insertion of the field
iS^{+}_{0}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})
in the Carrollian CFT correlators directly gives rise to the leading soft graviton theorem in the bulk (
3.8
) expressed for Carrollian amplitudes. One can further see that the relation (
4.29
) leads to the following expressions for the objects
S^{+}_{k(e)}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})
in terms of the (soft) graviton creation and annihilation operators:

\displaystyle S^{+}_{k(e)}(u\rightarrow\infty)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{1}{k!}\left(i\partial_{\omega}\right)^{k}\cdot\frac{\omega}{2}\left[\left\{a_{+}^{\text{out}}-{a_{+}^{\text{in}}}\right\}(\omega+i\delta)+(-)^{k}\left\{{a_{-}^{\text{out}}}^{\dagger}-{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}\right\}(\omega-i\delta)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\omega\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{1}{k!}\left(i\partial_{\omega}\right)^{k}\cdot\frac{\omega}{2}\left[a_{+}^{\text{out}}(\omega+i\delta)+(-)^{k+1}{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}(\omega-i\delta)\right]

(4.44)

where the last equality holds inside of the correlators. This is consistent with the earlier observation that the primary ‘Ward identity’
i\langle S_{k(e)}^{+}(\infty,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
describes the bulk tree-level (projected, for
k\geq 3
) (sub)
k
-leading soft graviton theorem.

4.6
More on the collinear limit

We shall now proceed to physically interpret the LHS of the (bulk) null-momentum space ‘OPE’ (
4.25
) in light of the momentum space representation (
4.42
) (suppressing the
i\delta
-prescription for brevity), stemming from the correspondence (
3.43
), of the Carrollian CFT primary field
S^{+}_{0}
. We recall that the RHS of the ‘OPE’ (
4.25
) has been recognized to be describing the leading order holomorphic collinear splitting (
4.26
) (with
\omega,\omega_{p}>0
), involving at least one positive helicity (in the ‘outgoing convention’) graviton, of the 4D Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitudes.

Choosing
\epsilon=\epsilon_{p}=1
in (
4.25
) and using
\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\omega>0)
(
4.42
), we get

\displaystyle\left[a_{+}^{\text{out}}-{a_{+}^{\text{in}}}\right](\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\sim-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\omega_{p}}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega_{p}+\omega}{\omega_{p}}\right)^{{s}_{p}}\text{ }\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\omega_{p}+\omega,z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}+\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}+\omega}\right)\,.

Since, the contribution of the
{a_{+}^{\text{in}}}
operator to this ‘OPE’ vanishes, we are left with

\displaystyle a_{+}^{\text{out}}(\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\sim-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\omega_{p}}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega_{p}+\omega}{\omega_{p}}\right)^{{s}_{p}}\text{ }\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\omega_{p}+\omega,z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}+\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}+\omega}\right)

(4.45)

that consistently describes the following holomorphic collinear splitting involving an outgoing positive helicity graviton in the 4D Einstein-Yang-Mills theory

\displaystyle{\Phi}^{*\text{out}}_{s_{p}}\left(\omega_{p}+\omega,z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}+\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}+\omega}\right)\longrightarrow{a_{+}^{\text{out}}}(\omega,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}^{\text{out}}_{s_{p}}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\,.

(4.46)

Similarly, choosing
\epsilon=-\epsilon_{p}=1
in (
4.25
) leads to (in the ‘all outgoing’ convention):

\displaystyle a_{+}^{\text{out}}(\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}(-\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\sim\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\omega_{p}}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega_{p}-\omega}{\omega_{p}}\right)^{{s}_{p}}\text{ }\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(-(\omega_{p}-\omega),z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}-\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}-\omega}\right)

(4.47)

by virtue of the vanishing contribution from
{a_{+}^{\text{in}}}
, which describes the following two 4D Einstein-Yang-Mills collinear splittings by crossing symmetry:

\displaystyle{\Phi}^{\text{in}\dagger}_{(-s_{p})}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\longrightarrow{a_{+}^{\text{out}}}(\omega,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}^{*\text{in}\dagger}_{(-s_{p})}\left(\omega_{p}-\omega,z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}-\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}-\omega}\right)\hskip 28.45274pt(\omega_{p}>\omega)\,,

(4.48)

\displaystyle{\Phi}^{*\text{out}}_{s_{p}}\left(\omega-\omega_{p},z_{p},\frac{\omega\bar{z}-\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}}{\omega-\omega_{p}}\right){\Phi}^{\text{in}\dagger}_{(-s_{p})}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\longrightarrow{a_{+}^{\text{out}}}(\omega,z,\bar{z})\hskip 28.45274pt(\omega>\omega_{p})\,.

Thus, we see that
\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\omega>0,z,\bar{z})
given by (
4.42
) naturally corresponds to the outgoing positive helicity graviton.

On the other hand, choosing
\epsilon=\epsilon_{p}=-1
in (
4.25
), using
\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\omega<0)
(
4.42
), and remembering that
{a_{+}^{\text{out}\dagger}}
does not contribute, one finds (with
a_{+}\equiv a_{+}^{\text{out}}
and
{a_{-}^{\text{in}\dagger}}(\omega)=a_{+}(-\omega)
for
\text{Re}(\omega)>0
, by crossing) that

\displaystyle a_{+}(-\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}(-\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\sim-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\omega_{p}}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega_{p}+\omega}{\omega_{p}}\right)^{{s}_{p}}\text{ }\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(-(\omega_{p}+\omega),z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}+\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}+\omega}\right)\,,

where all the energies and helicities are denoted in the ‘all outgoing’ convention in the last line, that describes the following collinear splitting involving an incoming negative helicity graviton in the 4D Einstein-Yang-Mills theory:

\displaystyle{a_{-}^{\text{in}\dagger}}(\omega,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}^{\text{in}\dagger}_{(-s_{p})}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\longrightarrow\Phi^{*\text{in}\dagger}_{(-s_{p})}\left(\omega_{p}+\omega,z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}+\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}+\omega}\right)\,.

This is the CPT transformed version of the splitting scenario (
4.46
), but with the same splitting coefficient (
4.45
). Similarly, the CPT transformations of the collinear splittings (
4.48
) lead to:

\displaystyle{a_{-}^{\text{in}\dagger}}(\omega,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}^{*\text{out}}_{s_{p}}\left(\omega_{p}-\omega,z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}-\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}-\omega}\right)\longrightarrow{\Phi}^{\text{out}}_{s_{p}}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\hskip 28.45274pt(\omega_{p}>\omega)\,,

\displaystyle{a_{-}^{\text{in}\dagger}}(\omega,z,\bar{z})\longrightarrow{\Phi}^{*\text{in}\dagger}_{(-s_{p})}\left(\omega-\omega_{p},z_{p},\frac{\omega\bar{z}-\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}}{\omega-\omega_{p}}\right){\Phi}^{\text{out}}_{s_{p}}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\hskip 28.45274pt(\omega>\omega_{p})

described by the same splitting coefficient (
4.47
) (in the ‘all outgoing’ convention):

\displaystyle a_{+}(-\omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}(\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})\sim\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\omega_{p}}{\omega}\left(\frac{\omega_{p}-\omega}{\omega_{p}}\right)^{{s}_{p}}\text{ }\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\omega_{p}-\omega,z_{p},\frac{\omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}-\omega\bar{z}}{\omega_{p}-\omega}\right)

(with
\omega>0
) which can be directly obtained by putting
\epsilon=-\epsilon_{p}=-1
in (
4.25
) and using
\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\omega<0)
(
4.42
). Hence,
\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\omega<0)
(
4.42
) corresponds to the incoming negative helicity graviton.

Thus, the momentum space ‘OPE’ (
4.25
) and its associated splitting scenario (
4.26
) contain the six Einstein-Yang-Mills holomorphic collinear splittings: (
4.46
), (
4.48
) and their CPT transformations, collectively given by, in the ‘all out’ convention,
with
\Omega_{i}\equiv\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}
:

\displaystyle{\Phi}^{*}_{s_{p}}\left(\Omega_{p}+\Omega,z_{p},\frac{\Omega_{p}\bar{z}_{p}+\Omega\bar{z}}{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}\right)\longrightarrow h_{+2}(\Omega,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}_{s_{p}}(\Omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}

\displaystyle a_{+}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}_{s_{p}}(\Omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

(4.49)

\displaystyle\sim\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}{\Omega+i\delta}\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}\right)^{{s}_{p}}{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\Omega_{p}+\Omega,z_{p},\frac{\left(\Omega_{p}-i\delta\right)\bar{z}_{p}+\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)\bar{z}}{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}\right)\,.

The above discussion also shows that the bulk CPT invariance and crossing relations naturally justifies the following regularization prescription for the binomial expansion in (
4.23
) outside its circle of convergence:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-2\bar{h}_{p})_{n}}{n!}\left(\frac{\Omega+i\delta}{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}\right)^{n-1}=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}{\Omega+i\delta}\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}\right)^{1-2\bar{h}_{p}}

(4.50)

in the context of the 4D bulk tree-level massless amplitude calculation in the ‘all outgoing’ convention
16
16

16

Alternatively, since the amplitudes are actually distributions due to the presence of the momentum-conserving delta-functions, (
4.50
) may be thought to be valid in a distributional sense. Explicit checks with bulk amplitudes in Appendix
G.2
confirm this interpretation.
such that the ‘OPE’ (
4.25
) is expressed as (with
1-2\bar{h}_{p}=s_{p}
):

\displaystyle-i\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\Omega,z,\bar{z}){\Phi}_{s_{p}}(\Omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}{\Omega+i\delta}\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}\right)^{{s}_{p}}{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\Omega_{p}+\Omega,z_{p},\frac{\left(\Omega_{p}-i\delta\right)\bar{z}_{p}+\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)\bar{z}}{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}\right)

(4.51)

which is valid for any
\Omega,\Omega_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
(the variables
\Omega
and
\Omega_{p}
are interpreted as (bilateral) Fourier conjugates to
u
and
u_{p}
). Moreover, the validity of the soft graviton expansion (
4.19
) includes both
\Omega\rightarrow 0^{\pm}
:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\Omega\rightarrow 0}i\left\langle\tilde{S}_{0}^{+}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle=\lim\limits_{\Omega\rightarrow 0}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\left[\frac{F^{(0)}}{\Omega+i\delta}+\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}{F^{(k+1)}}{\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)^{k}}\right]\left\langle\tilde{X}_{\text{out}}\tilde{X}_{\text{in}}\right\rangle

with the
\Omega\rightarrow 0^{-}(0^{+})
limit denoting the incoming negative (outgoing positive) helicity soft graviton theorem. The above expansion can then be generated solely by an insertion of the operator
\lim\limits_{\Omega\rightarrow 0}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}-a_{+}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})
into the 4D
\mathcal{S}
-matrix. Hence, as a consequence of the CPT invariance and crossing symmetry of the 4D bulk gravity theory, the insertions of the operators
-i\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})
and
\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}a_{+}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})
(with
\Omega\in\mathbb{R}
) into a bulk massless
\mathcal{S}
-matrix are equivalent to each other,
leading to the following correspondence in the ‘all outgoing’ convention:

\displaystyle\tilde{S}^{+}_{0}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}ia_{+}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})\hskip 11.38109pt(\text{for }\Omega\in\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 5.69054pt{{S}}^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\Omega u}\text{ }a_{+}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})

(4.52)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\left[e^{-i\omega u}\text{ }a_{+}^{\text{out}}(\omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})+e^{i\omega u}\text{ }{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}(\omega-i\delta,z,\bar{z})\right]\,.

Note that this is completely equivalent to the correspondence (
4.41
) at the level of bulk
\mathcal{S}
-matrix insertion, since
{a_{+}^{\text{in}}}
and
{a_{-}^{\text{out}}}^{\dagger}
do not contribute to the
\mathcal{S}
-matrix.
Thus, (
4.52
) establishes the direct correspondence between the conformal Carrollian field
{S}^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})
and the 4D positive (in the ‘outgoing’ sense i.e. both outgoing positive and incoming negative) helicity graviton. The correspondence in (
4.52
) is also consistent with the conventions chosen in Section 11 of
[
47
]
, where the Fourier integral is performed over all real values.

Furthermore, we note from the relation (
4.24
) (valid for
\Omega\in\mathbb{R}
) and the correspondence (
4.52
) that:

\displaystyle\tilde{{S}}^{+}_{k}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{\tilde{{S}}^{+}_{0}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})}{\left(-i\Omega+\delta\right)^{k}}=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{ia_{+}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})}{\left(-i\Omega+\delta\right)^{k}}\hskip 14.22636pt(\text{for }\Omega\in\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle{S}^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\Omega u}\text{ }\frac{a_{+}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z})}{\left(-i\Omega+\delta\right)^{k}}\,.

(4.53)

With the temporal boundary condition for the field
{S}^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})
unspecified, the above correspondence is formally generalized as (for
\Omega\in\mathbb{R}-\{0\}
):

\displaystyle\tilde{{S}}^{+}_{k}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})=\frac{ia_{+}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})}{\left(-i\Omega\right)^{k}}\hskip 7.11317pt\Longleftrightarrow\hskip 7.11317pt{S}^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})=\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\Omega u}\text{ }\frac{ia_{+}(\Omega,z,\bar{z})}{\left(-i\Omega\right)^{k}}

(4.54)

where the
\Omega
-integral is understood in the principal value sense.

In Appendix
G
, we illustrate the definitions and the results presented in this section on explicit examples of massless scattering amplitudes.

5
Discussion

In this paper, using the Carrollian approach to flat space holography, we showed that the large
u
limit of the Carrollian CFT stress tensor Ward identities correctly reproduces the leading and subleading soft graviton theorems in the bulk. This allowed us to explicitly express the Carrollian stress tensor in terms of the hard graviton operators, and thus the bulk radiative modes. The universality of the leading and subleading soft theorems reflects the presence of a stress tensor as part of the definition of the holographic Carrollian CFT. Moreover, we showed that the inclusion of an operator
S^{+}_{2}
in the Carrollian CFT, from which a whole tower of
Lw_{1+\infty}
currents automatically follows, implies an infinite hierarchy of projected soft graviton theorems for Einstein gravity in the bulk in the
u\to\infty
limit. We also demonstrated that the
Lw_{1+\infty}
currents coincide with an infinite tower of
\partial_{u}
-Carrollian ascendants of the hard graviton operator, and their OPEs at finite
u
correctly encode the collinear limit of scattering amplitudes in the bulk. In particular, the fact that the stress tensor at finite
u
encodes information about the bulk hard graviton is reminiscent of the AdS/CFT correspondence, where stress tensor correlation functions in the CFT coincide with bulk graviton correlators.

Let us emphasize that, in our approach, we began with the properties of the Carrollian CFT to deduce aspects of the bulk theory—opposite to the approach commonly used in the literature, which typically translates amplitude statements into the dual theory. This allows us to have more control over the properties the Carrollian CFT should satisfy, such as OPE associativity or constraints on the spectrum.

In the following, we relate our results with previous analyses in the literature.

Sourced Ward identities

In
[
43
,
45
]
, it was shown that sourced Ward identities for a Carrollian CFT stress tensor reproduce the leading and subleading soft graviton theorems. Our current analysis does not require the introduction of external sources but is nevertheless compatible with this previous result. Indeed, the stress tensor considered there was constructed from the non-radiative subsector of the gravitational solution space (inspired by results in 3D gravity
[
34
,
109
,
110
,
40
,
111
,
112
]
) and encodes information on the Bondi mass and angular momentum aspects. This subsector is typically used to describe bound states. External sources with respect to this subsector were required to capture the radiation at null infinity (see also the discussion section of
[
45
]
for a summary). In the present work, we focus on the complementary subsector of 4D gravity—specifically, the ‘pure radiation’ sector describing massless scattering. In particular, the ‘source operators’ with respect to the non-radiative phase space considered in
[
45
]
are precisely the Carrollian primary operators for the massless scattering states considered here. Thus, the stress tensor constructed in this work should not be compared to the stress tensor considered in these earlier references, as they describe different subsectors. One of the important challenges of flat space holography is to understand the interplay between these two bulk subsectors–massless scattering and bound states–from a holographic perspective.

The large
u
limit

In this work, we have shown that the large
u
limit in the Carrollian CFT coincides with the soft limit in the bulk theory. This is physically intuitive, as the observation of soft/large wavelength particles at null infinity requires considering large time intervals at
\mathscr{I}
. This led us to the observation that, at finite time, the Carrollian CFT Ward identities encode not only the soft theorems but also the collinear factorization properties of bulk scattering amplitudes. Although similar in appearance, the approach used here differs from the one proposed very recently in
[
113
]
(based on
[
114
]
). One key difference is that we consider the quantum Ward identities associated with the Carrollian CFT stress tensor to reproduce the soft theorems. By contrast, in
[
113
]
, the stress tensor is obtained by solving classical Carrollian constraints/time evolution equations for the stress tensor (thereby omitting the contact terms arising from the insertion of Carrollian primaries in the correlators), and the time-independent integration functions on the celestial sphere are promoted as components of the Carrollian CFT stress tensor operator at
\mathscr{I}
. In fact, the stress tensor considered there is essentially the integrated BMS flux momenta discussed in
[
97
]
. These objects are non-local at
\mathscr{I}
when expressed in terms of the asymptotic shear and naturally live on the celestial sphere. Indeed, they can be viewed as corners of the celestial diamonds discussed in
[
115
,
116
]
. Therefore, the analysis in
[
113
]
does not capture the finite
u
information of the Carrollian CFT Ward identities—namely, the hard graviton operators—but only the soft subsector. Moreover, as discussed in Section
3.4
(and also in Appendix
A
), the quantum symmetries of the holographic Carrollian CFT (
3.38
) imply
{T^{z}}_{\bar{z}}=0
(together with
{T^{\bar{z}}}_{z}=0
if we include all the diffeomorphisms on the celestial sphere for superrotations). Thus, these stress tensor components do not encode further information about the hard graviton.

Let us conclude with some perspectives. First, soft photon theorems for Carrollian amplitudes have been recently derived in
[
49
,
50
]
from conformal Carrollian partition functions at
\mathscr{I}
. It would be interesting to explore whether the results obtained here can be directly derived from the Carrollian CFT partition function for gravity.

Additionally, it would be important to establish an independent definition of the Carrollian CFT that captures the features of scattering amplitudes in flat space. Preliminary steps have been made in this direction by taking Carrollian limits of holographic CFTs. For example,
[
81
]
considers the limit at the level of holographic CFT correlators, while
[
117
]
examines the limit at the level of the Lagrangian. Furthermore, Carrollian CFT correlators have been computed from an intrinsic perspective in
[
118
]
. Based on the discussion in Section
3.5
, twistor theory appears to be the natural framework for formulating a holographic Carrollian CFT. An intriguing question is how the results obtained in this paper might emerge in the flat space limit of AdS/CFT limit. We leave these questions for future investigation.

workshop Carrollian Physics and Holography, where part of this project has been carried out.
