This work was also supported by the Simons Collaboration on Celestial Holography. AS is supported by the Prime Minister’s Research Fellowship, MHRD, India.

Appendix A
The 3D Carrollian CFT stress tensor

To shed light on the intrinsic Carrollian derivation of the stress tensor Ward identities (
2.10
)-(
2.13
), we discuss in this appendix on the classical aspects of the stress tensor in a generic 3D field theory on a flat Carrollian background (with topology
\mathbb{R}\times S^{2}
), with its action possessing the original BMS
4
(
\cong\text{Lorentz}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\displaystyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\textstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[8.36108pt]{\raisebox{-0.83334pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}{\makebox[7.36115pt]{\raisebox{-0.83336pt}{\begin{picture}\scriptscriptstyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}
\put(0.5,0.5){\arc[270,90]{0.5}}
\end{picture}}}}}\text{Supertranslations}
) algebra
[
60
,
61
]
as the kinematical symmetry. We shall closely follow the analogous discussion on the 2D Carrollian CFT stress tensor presented in
[
85
]
.

To set the stage, we first review the definitions of the relevant entities from a classical field theory. Suppose that under the infinitesimal transformation:

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\rightarrow x^{\prime\mu}=x^{\mu}+\epsilon^{a}\zeta^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})

(A.1)

a multi-component field transforms as a finite matrix representation as (
i
denotes the matrix representation index):

\displaystyle\Phi^{i}(\mathbf{x})\rightarrow{\tilde{\Phi}}^{i}(\mathbf{x^{\prime}})=\Phi^{i}(\mathbf{x})+\epsilon^{a}{(\mathcal{F}_{a}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}(\mathbf{x})\,.

(A.2)

Defining the transformation generators as
[
119
]
:

\displaystyle\delta_{\bm{\epsilon}}\Phi^{i}(\mathbf{x})\equiv{\tilde{\Phi}}^{i}(\mathbf{x})-{{\Phi}}^{i}(\mathbf{x}):=-i\epsilon^{a}G_{a}(\mathbf{x})\Phi^{i}(\mathbf{x})\equiv-i\epsilon^{a}G_{a}\Phi^{i}(\mathbf{x})

the generator
G_{a}(\mathbf{x})
of the above transformation is found to be:

\displaystyle-iG_{a}(\mathbf{x})\Phi^{i}(\mathbf{x})={(\mathcal{F}_{a}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}(\mathbf{x})-\zeta^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i}(\mathbf{x})\,.

(A.3)

The following action describing the classical theory of the fields
\mathbf{\Phi}
:

\displaystyle S[\bm{\Phi}]=\int d^{d+1}\mathbf{x}\text{ }\mathcal{L}(\bm{\Phi},\partial_{\mu}\bm{\Phi})

(A.4)

transforms on-shell under the above transformation as
[
119
]
:

\displaystyle S[\bm{\Phi}]\rightarrow S^{\prime}[\bm{\tilde{\Phi}}]=S[\bm{\Phi}]+\int d^{d+1}\mathbf{x}\text{ }\epsilon^{a}\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})

with
j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})
being the corresponding Noether current (summation over the repeated indices
i
are implicit):

\displaystyle j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})={T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\nu}\zeta^{\nu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}-{(\mathcal{F}_{a}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\,.

(A.5)

Here,
{T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\nu}
is the canonical stress tensor which is the (on-shell) conserved Noether current associated to the global spacetime translation symmetry. More generally, if the transformation induced by the generator (
A.3
) is a symmetry of the action (
A.4
), the corresponding Noether current (
A.5
) is on-shell conserved:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}=0\,.

Turning to the 3D Carrollian CFT, we now note that the infinitesimal version of the original BMS
4
transformations is given by:

\displaystyle z\rightarrow z+\epsilon\mathcal{Y}(z)\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317pt\bar{z}\rightarrow\bar{z}+\bar{\epsilon}\bar{\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{z})\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317ptu\rightarrow u\left(1+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(z)+\frac{\bar{\epsilon}}{2}\bar{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\right)+\epsilon^{u}\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})

(A.6)

with
\mathcal{Y}(z)
and
\bar{\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{z})
being at most quadratic polynomials of
z
and
\bar{z}
respectively, with the restrictions
\bar{z}=z^{*}
,
\bar{\epsilon}=\epsilon^{*}
and
\bar{\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{z})=\mathcal{Y}(z)^{*}
, while
\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})
is an arbitrary function restricted to be real. We shall keep treating
z
and
\bar{z}
as independent variables only to impose the said restrictions at the end.

As discussed in
[
46
]
, the generator of the infinitesimal supertranslation
\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})
induces the following transformation on a 3D classical conformal Carrollian primary multiplet
\Phi
:

\displaystyle-iG_{\mathcal{T}}{\Phi}(u,z,\bar{z})=-\left[\mathcal{T}\partial_{u}+\left(\partial_{z}\mathcal{T}\right)\bm{\xi}+\left(\partial_{\bar{z}}\mathcal{T}\right)\bar{\bm{\xi}}\right]\cdot{\Phi}(u,z,\bar{z})

(A.7)

with
\bm{\xi}
and
\bar{\bm{\xi}}
furnishing a finite-dimensional indecomposable but reducible representation of the two 3D Carrollian boosts. If the field
\Phi
transforms under the 3D Carrollian spin-boost irrep, it must have
\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi=\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi=0
. On the other hand, a Lorentz tensor (or
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
(quasi-)primary) field
\Phi
undergoes the following infinitesimal Lorentz transformations
[
46
]
:

\displaystyle-iG_{\mathcal{Y}}{\Phi}(u,z,\bar{z})=-\left[\mathcal{Y}(z)\partial_{z}+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(z)\left(\mathbf{h}+\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}\right)+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}(z)\frac{u}{2}\bm{\xi}\right]\cdot{\Phi}(u,z,\bar{z})\,,

(A.8)

\displaystyle-iG_{\bar{\mathcal{Y}}}{\Phi}(u,z,\bar{z})=-\left[\bar{\mathcal{Y}}(\bar{z})\partial_{\bar{z}}+\bar{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}(\bar{z})\left(\bar{\mathbf{h}}+\frac{u}{2}\partial_{u}\right)+\bar{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{z})\frac{u}{2}\bar{\bm{\xi}}\right]\cdot{\Phi}(u,z,\bar{z})\,,

(A.9)

with
\mathbf{h},\bar{\mathbf{h}}=\frac{\Delta\mathbf{I}\pm\mathbf{J}}{2}
. The 3D conformal Carrollian multiplets with covariant transformation properties (
A.7
)-(
A.9
) under the original BMS
4
group will be referred to as the BMS
4
primary fields.

We now consider a 3D classical field theory on flat Carrollian background with topology
\mathbb{R}\times S^{2}
, described by the following action
[
120
]
:

\displaystyle S[\bm{\Phi}]=\int du\int\limits_{S^{2}}d^{2}\vec{x}\text{ }\mathcal{L}(\bm{\Phi},\partial_{\mu}\bm{\Phi})

(A.10)

which we demand to be (original) BMS
4
invariant. Treating the celestial sphere
S^{2}
as the Riemann sphere, we use the (mostly) flat metric on it. Following the relativistic case
[
121
]
, the ‘fundamental’ fields appearing in the Lagrangian are assumed to be BMS
4
primaries.

Then, the Noether current associated with the
\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})
supertranslation is given below, from (
A.5
) and (
A.7
), as:

\displaystyle j^{\mu}_{\text{ST}}={T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}\mathcal{T}+\left[(\partial_{z}\mathcal{T}){(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}+(\partial_{\bar{z}}\mathcal{T}){(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\right]\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\,.

(A.11)

Since this supertranslation is demanded to be a symmetry of the Carrollian CFT action, the above Noether current is on-shell conserved. Its conservation law leads to the following condition on the stress tensor as well as on the Lagrangian itself since
\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})
is arbitrary:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}_{\text{ST}}=0\hskip 7.11317pt\text{(on-shell)}\text{ }\Longrightarrow\text{ }

\displaystyle{T_{(c)}}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}+\partial_{\mu}\left({(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\right)=0\,,

(A.12)

\displaystyle{T_{(c)}}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}+\partial_{\mu}\left({(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\right)=0\,,

(A.13)

\displaystyle{({\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{z}\Phi^{i})}=

\displaystyle{(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\bar{z}}\Phi^{i})}={(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{z}\Phi^{i})}+{({\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\bar{z}}\Phi^{i})}=0\,.

(A.14)

The constraints (
A.14
) are hence necessary for a 3D Carrollian CFT Lagrangian to satisfy (on-shell) if the action is to be supertranslation invariant. The on-shell conservation of the stress tensor
{T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}
, together with the on-shell conditions (
A.12
)-(
A.14
) then implies:

\displaystyle\partial_{u}{T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}-\partial_{u}\partial_{z}\left({(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)-\partial_{u}\partial_{\bar{z}}\left({(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)=0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(on-shell)}\,.

(A.15)

Thus, the stress tensor of a 3D Carrollian CFT can be Belinfante-improved (off-shell), as the consequence of the conditions (
A.12
), (
A.13
) and (
A.15
) stemming from the supertranslation invariance, as below (
{T}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\nu}
is the improved stress tensor):

\displaystyle{T}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}={T}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}=0\hskip 5.69054pt,\hskip 5.69054pt

\displaystyle{T}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}={T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}-\partial_{z}\left({(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)-\partial_{\bar{z}}\left({(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)\hskip 7.11317pt\text{(off-shell)}

(A.16)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 7.11317pt

\displaystyle\partial_{u}{T}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}=0\hskip 7.11317pt\text{(on-shell)}

such that
{T}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}
remains on-shell conserved. It is the improvement
{T}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}={T}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}=0
that allows us to re-express the conserved supertranslation currents
j^{\mu}_{\text{ST}}
into the following simple form:

\displaystyle j^{\mu}_{\text{ST}}={T}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}\cdot\mathcal{T}(z,\bar{z})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(off-shell)}\,.

(A.17)

We now investigate the consequences of the
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
(Lorentz) invariance of the 3D Carrollian CFT action. It turns out that, for this purpose, we can safely treat
z
and
\bar{z}
as independent variables without altering any results. From (
A.5
) and (
A.8
), the Noether current corresponding to the holomorphic Lorentz
\mathcal{Y}(z)
invariance is found to be (with
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z)=0
):

\displaystyle j^{\mu}_{\text{HL}}=\mathcal{Y}(z){T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}+\frac{u}{2}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(z){T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}+\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}(z){(\mathbf{h}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}+\frac{u}{2}\mathcal{Y}^{\prime\prime}(z){(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\,.

(A.18)

Conservation of this current implies the following on-shell conditions:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}_{\text{HL}}=0\hskip 7.11317pt\text{(on-shell)}\text{ }\Longrightarrow\text{ }

\displaystyle{T_{(c)}}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}+\frac{1}{2}{T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}+\partial_{\mu}\left({(\mathbf{h}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\right)=0\,,

(A.19)

\displaystyle{(\mathbf{h}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{z}\Phi^{i})}+\frac{1}{2}{(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}=0\,,

(A.20)

along with the known relation (
A.12
). Similarly, from the anti-holomorphic Lorentz invariance, we obtain the ‘complex-conjugates’ of the above conditions:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}_{\text{AL}}=0\hskip 7.11317pt\text{(on-shell)}\text{ }\Longrightarrow\text{ }

\displaystyle{T_{(c)}}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}+\frac{1}{2}{T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}+\partial_{\mu}\left({(\bar{\mathbf{h}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\right)=0\,,

(A.21)

\displaystyle{(\bar{\mathbf{h}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\bar{z}}\Phi^{i})}+\frac{1}{2}{(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}=0\,.

(A.22)

The restriction (
A.20
) and (
A.22
) are the necessary on-shell conditions for the Lorentz invariance of the action.

Now, using the improvement (
A.15
) and the restrictions (
A.20
) and (
A.22
), we reach from the on-shell relation (
A.19
) to:

\displaystyle{T_{(c)}}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}+\frac{1}{2}T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}+\partial_{u}\left({(\mathbf{h}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)+\partial_{\bar{z}}\left({(\mathbf{J}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\bar{z}}\Phi^{i})}\right)=0\,.

Thus, one can Belinfante-improve the
{T_{(c)}}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}
component as below:

\displaystyle T^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}={T_{(c)}}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}+\partial_{u}\left({(\mathbf{h}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)+\partial_{\bar{z}}\left({(\mathbf{J}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\bar{z}}\Phi^{i})}\right)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(off-shell)}

(A.23)

leading to the following on-shell relation:

\displaystyle T^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}+\frac{1}{2}T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}=0\,.

Similarly, starting from (
A.21
) and improving the
{T_{(c)}}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}
component as:

\displaystyle T^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}={T_{(c)}}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}+\partial_{u}\left({(\bar{\mathbf{h}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)-\partial_{{z}}\left({(\mathbf{J}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{{z}}\Phi^{i})}\right)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(off-shell)}

(A.24)

we are finally led to the on-shell symmetric, trace-less nature of the improved stress tensor:

\displaystyle T^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}=T^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}=-\frac{1}{2}T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}\hskip 14.22636pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636ptT^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\mu}=0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(on-shell)}\,.

(A.25)

It follows from (
A.23
) and the conservation of
{T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}
that the components
{T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}
and
{T_{(c)}}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}
can be (off-shell) improved as below:

\displaystyle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}={T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}-\partial_{z}\left({({\mathbf{h}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)\hskip 14.22636pt;\hskip 14.22636ptT^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}={T_{(c)}}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}-\partial_{z}\left({({\mathbf{J}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\bar{z}}\Phi^{i})}\right)

(A.26)

and similarly, for
{T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{\bar{z}}}
and
{T_{(c)}}^{{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}
, one has:

\displaystyle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}={T_{(c)}}^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}-\partial_{\bar{z}}\left({(\bar{\mathbf{h}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{u}\Phi^{i})}\right)\hskip 14.22636pt;\hskip 14.22636ptT^{{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}={T_{(c)}}^{{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}+\partial_{\bar{z}}\left({({\mathbf{J}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{{z}}\Phi^{i})}\right)

(A.27)

such that both
T^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}
and
T^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}
remain conserved on-shell.

Thus, we have shown, as the consequences of the Lorentz and supertranslation invariance, that the stress tensor in a 3D classical Carrollian CFT can be improved to become (on-shell) symmetric in spatial indices and trace-less (
A.25
) along with the (off-shell) vanishing of the components
T^{i}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{u}}
(
A.16
). These conclusions follow even when the assumption that the fields appearing in the Lagrangian transform as BMS
4
primary fields is given up; no knowledge of the specific BMS
4
transformation properties of the ‘fundamental’ fields is required. Due to those symmetry properties of the stress tensor, the conserved Noether current associated with any original BMS
4
symmetry-transformation
x^{\mu}\rightarrow x^{\mu}+\epsilon^{a}\zeta^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})
can be expressed in the following simple form:

\displaystyle j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}={T}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\nu}\zeta^{\nu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(off-shell)}\,.

(A.28)

Intriguingly, no additional condition or restriction on the Lagrangians other than (
A.12
)-(
A.14
) and (
A.19
)-(
A.22
) arises if we demand invariance of the action under arbitrary superrotations contained in the extended BMS
4
group (even when
z
and
\bar{z}
are kept completely independent):

\displaystyle z\rightarrow z+\epsilon z^{n+1}\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317pt\bar{z}\rightarrow\bar{z}\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317ptu\rightarrow u+\epsilon\frac{u}{2}(n+1)z^{n}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(holomorphic superrotation)}

\displaystyle z\rightarrow z\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317pt\bar{z}\rightarrow\bar{z}+\epsilon\bar{z}^{n+1}\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317ptu\rightarrow u+\epsilon\frac{u}{2}(n+1)\bar{z}^{n}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(anti-holomorphic superrotation)}

where
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, the classical
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
Lorentz and supertranslation symmetries of the 3D Carrollian CFT action
automatically
lead to its invariance under an infinite number of arbitrary superrotations, implying the extended BMS
4
symmetry of the action. This symmetry-enhancement of the classical action of 3D Carrollian CFT from the original (global) BMS
4
to the extended (local) BMS
4
or, more precisely, from the global
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
(Lorentz) part thereof to the local superrotations, is perfectly analogous to the enhancement of the global conformal symmetry to the local infinite-dimensional one in a 2D CFT. By virtue of the properties (
A.16
) and (
A.25
) of the improved stress tensor, each of the conserved Noether currents corresponding to the extended BMS
4
symmetry can be (off-shell) expressed in the form (
A.28
).

On the other hand, the modes contained in the object
S^{+}_{1e}(u,z,\bar{z})
generate the following infinitesimal Carrollian diffeomorphisms on the Hilbert space
[
86
]
:

\displaystyle z\rightarrow z\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317pt\bar{z}\rightarrow\bar{z}+\epsilon z^{n}\bar{z}^{q+1}\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317ptu\rightarrow u+\epsilon\frac{u}{2}(q+1)z^{n}\bar{z}^{q}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{(with $q=0,\pm 1$ and $n\in\mathbb{Z}$)}

(A.29)

under which a 3D conformal Carrollian primary multiplet
\Phi
transforms as
[
46
]
:

\displaystyle\left[j^{(-)}_{n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]=-iz_{p}^{n}\partial_{\bar{z}_{p}}\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

\displaystyle\left[j^{(0)}_{n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]=-i\left[z^{n}_{p}\left(\bar{z}_{p}\partial_{\bar{z}_{p}}+\frac{u_{p}}{2}\partial_{u_{p}}+\bar{\mathbf{h}}_{p}\right)+nz^{n-1}_{p}\frac{u_{p}}{2}\bm{\xi}_{p}\right]\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

(A.30)

\displaystyle\left[j^{(+)}_{n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]=-i\left[z_{p}^{n}\left(\bar{z}^{2}_{p}\partial_{\bar{z}_{p}}+\bar{z}_{p}u_{p}\partial_{u_{p}}+2\bar{z}_{p}\bar{\mathbf{h}}_{p}+u_{p}\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}_{p}\right)+nz_{p}^{n-1}\bar{z}_{p}{u_{p}}\bm{\xi}_{p}\right]\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

Since the modes
j^{(a)}_{n}
generate a symmetry at the level of the Carrollian CFT OPE, the action (
A.10
) should be invariant under the diffeomorphisms (
A.29
). From (
A.5
) and (
A.30
), the Noether currents associated to these diffeomorphisms are given as:

\displaystyle j^{\mu}_{\text{Diff}}

\displaystyle=z^{n}\bar{z}^{q+1}{T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}+\frac{u}{2}(q+1)z^{n}\bar{z}^{q}{T_{(c)}}^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}+(q+1)z^{n}\bar{z}^{q}{(\bar{\mathbf{h}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{u}{2}(q+1)qz^{n}\bar{z}^{q-1}{(\bar{\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}+\frac{u}{2}(q+1)nz^{n-1}\bar{z}^{q}{({\bm{\xi}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i})}\,.

(A.31)

The conservation of these diffeomorphism currents, together with the (original) BMS
4
invariance conditions (
A.12
)-(
A.14
) and (
A.19
)-(
A.22
), impose the following additional (on-shell) restrictions on the Lagrangian:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}_{\text{Diff}}=0\hskip 7.11317pt\text{(on-shell)}\text{ }\Longrightarrow\text{ }{T_{(c)}}^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}={({\mathbf{J}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{{z}}\Phi^{i})}=0

(A.32)

which keep even the improved component
T^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}
fixed at
0
off-shell, as seen from (
A.27
). In fact, if we extend the transformations (
A.29
) to include any arbitrary diffeomorphisms on the sphere
[
88
,
89
,
90
]
as the symmetries of the action (
A.10
), we also recover the ‘conjugate’ restrictions in a similar way:

\displaystyle{T_{(c)}}^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}={({\mathbf{J}}\cdot\Phi)}^{i}\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial(\partial_{\bar{z}}\Phi^{i})}=0

(A.33)

which persist upon improvement:
T^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}=0
(off-shell), from (
A.26
). The Noether currents corresponding to the Carrollian diffeomorphisms can then all be expressed in the form (
A.28
).

Assuming only the classical (original) BMS
4
invariance of the action (
A.10
), we now sketch the derivation of the stress tensor Ward identities in the corresponding QFT in the Carrollian path-integral formalism
[
74
]
. In this formalism, an
n
-point correlator (covariant time-ordered) is defined as (suppressing the field tensor indices):

\displaystyle\langle X\rangle\equiv\langle\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x_{1}})\Phi_{2}(\mathbf{x_{2}})...\Phi_{n}(\mathbf{x_{n}})\rangle:=\frac{\int[\mathcal{D}\bm{\Phi}]\text{ }\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x_{1}})\Phi_{2}(\mathbf{x_{2}})...\Phi_{n}(\mathbf{x_{n}})\text{ }e^{iS[\bm{\Phi}]}}{\int[\mathcal{D}\bm{{\Phi}}]\text{ }e^{iS[\bm{{\Phi}}]}}\,.

(A.34)

A field transformation, e.g. (
A.2
), will be a (local) symmetry of the QFT if, for any
X
:

\displaystyle\langle X\rangle={\langle X\rangle}^{\prime}\equiv\langle\tilde{\Phi}_{1}(\mathbf{x_{1}})\tilde{\Phi}_{2}(\mathbf{x_{2}})...\tilde{\Phi}_{n}(\mathbf{x_{n}})\rangle:=\frac{\int[\mathcal{D}\bm{\tilde{\Phi}}]\text{ }\tilde{\Phi}_{1}(\mathbf{x_{1}})\tilde{\Phi}_{2}(\mathbf{x_{2}})...\tilde{\Phi}_{n}(\mathbf{x_{n}})\text{ }e^{iS^{\prime}[\bm{\tilde{\Phi}}]}}{\int[\mathcal{D}\bm{\tilde{\Phi}}]\text{ }e^{iS^{\prime}[\bm{\tilde{\Phi}}]}}\,.

(A.35)

Under the assumption of invariance of the path-integral measure:
[\mathcal{D}\bm{\Phi}]=[\mathcal{D}\bm{\tilde{\Phi}}]
, this symmetry condition, at the 1st order in
\bm{\epsilon}
, leads to the Ward identity
[
119
]
:

\displaystyle-\delta_{\bm{\epsilon}}\langle X\rangle\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x_{1}}))...(i\epsilon^{a}G_{a}\Phi_{i}(\mathbf{x_{i}}))...\Phi_{n}(\mathbf{x_{n}})\rangle=i\int du\text{ }d^{2}\vec{x}\text{ }\epsilon^{a}\langle\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})\left(X-\langle X\rangle\right)\rangle\,.

(A.36)

In the 3D Carrollian CFTs, the Ward identity takes the following differential form when
X
is a string of conformal Carrollian primary fields, up to derivatives of temporal contact-terms:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\langle j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})(X-\langle X\rangle)\rangle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\text{ }\delta(u-u_{i})\delta^{2}(\vec{x}-\vec{x_{i}}){{(G_{a})}_{i}\langle X\rangle}\,.

(A.37)

To reach the stress tensor Ward identities (
2.10
)-(
2.13
) from here, all we need is the form (
A.28
) of the Noether currents
j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}
and the fact that
\partial_{\mu}\langle j^{\mu}_{\hskip 4.2679pta}(\mathbf{x})\rangle=0
. Had
X
been a string of arbitrary conformal Carrollian non-primary fields, derivatives of the spatial delta-function would have also appeared on the RHS of these Ward identities.

Now, the Carrollian diffeomorphisms (
A.29
) are local symmetries of the 3D quantum Carrollian CFT. As a consequence, we have the following trivial Ward identity:
\langle T^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}(\mathbf{x})X\rangle=0
, which can be taken to be exact. Thus, the component
T^{z}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}
vanishes both at the classical as well as the quantum level. If the Lagrangian is hermitian, the ‘conjugate’ component
T^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}
also has to vanish.

Appendix B
OPE formulae

OPE formulae for
S_{0}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})

We now discuss the Carrollian OPE between
S_{0}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})
and a general Carrollian operator (primary or descendent). These formulae will be useful when extending the analysis to
Lw_{1+\infty}
in Section
4.1
.

We will treat the coordinates
z
and
\bar{z}

as independent variables so that any term like
\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{r}}{\left(z-z_{p}\right)^{s}}
with
r\geq 0,s\geq 1
has (meromorphic) pole singularity. Together with this, the form of the Ward identity (
3.17
) is suggestive of the below decomposition of
S^{+}_{0}
inside the correlator, following
[
86
]
:

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})=\bar{z}P_{-1}(u,z,\bar{z})-P_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})

(B.1)

with

\displaystyle\langle P_{-1}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=-i\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\text{ }\theta(u-u_{p})\left(\frac{\partial_{u_{p}}}{z-z_{p}}+\frac{\bm{\xi}_{p}}{{(z-z_{p})}^{2}}\right)\langle X\rangle

and

\displaystyle\langle P_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=-i\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\text{ }\theta(u-u_{p})\left(\frac{\bar{z}_{p}\partial_{u_{p}}+\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}_{p}}{z-z_{p}}+\frac{\bar{z}_{p}\bm{\xi}_{p}}{{(z-z_{p})}^{2}}\right)\langle X\rangle

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\partial}P_{i}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=-i\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\text{ }\theta(u-u_{p})\text{ }\left[\text{contact terms on $S^{2}$}\right]\,.

These relations are reminiscent of the holomorphic Kač-Moody like Ward identities in a 2D Euclidean CFT. Both
P_{i}
and
S^{+}_{0}
clearly have the same holomorphic weight
h=\frac{3}{2}
. For later use, we introduce the mode decomposition

P_{i}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}P_{n,i}z^{-n-2}\,,\qquad i=0,-1

(B.2)

where the modes have been re-labelled such that
n
takes integer values.

Below, we note the OPE of
S^{+}_{0}
with a general conformal Carrollian (not necessarily primary) multiplet
\Phi
(that is mutually local with
S^{+}_{0}
) in the
j\epsilon
-form
[
46
]
:

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}

\displaystyle-i\left[(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})\left(\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}^{J}\frac{\left(P_{n,-1}\Phi\right)}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{n+2}}+\frac{\partial_{u_{p}}\Phi}{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}\right)-\sum\limits_{n\geq-1}^{K}\frac{\left(P_{n,0}\Phi\right)}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{n+2}}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})

(B.3)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}^{2}S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0

(B.4)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{u}S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0

(B.5)

where (especially on the first line)
\sim
denotes ‘modulo terms holomorphic (regular) in
\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}
’, and
K
,
J
are integers depending on the particular field. This OPE is clearly a Laurent expansion in the holomorphic variable
z
(or
\tilde{z}=z-j\epsilon u
) but is regular in
\bar{z}
. The relations (
B.4
) and (
B.5
) are, on the other hand, exact as OPE statements since all the contact terms in the RHS vanish in the OPE limit.

While denoting the residues of the higher order poles in the OPE (
B.3
), we have followed the ‘normal-ordering’ notation introduced in section 6.5 of
[
119
]
. For a conformal Carrollian primary multiplet
\Phi
as defined in (
A.9
), we specially have:

\displaystyle\left(P_{n+1,-1}\Phi\right)=0=\left(P_{n,0}\Phi\right)\text{\hskip 14.22636pt for $n\geq 0$}\,.

(B.6)

The OPE involving a 3D conformal Carrollian descendant multiplet can be obtained following a standard 2D CFT like method, starting from the OPE of its parent primary field. The higher order residues are then readily extracted by comparison with the general OPE (
B.3
). A 3D conformal Carrollian quasi-primary (i.e. merely an
\mathfrak{iso}(3,1)
covariant) multiplet needs to only satisfy
\left(P_{0,0}\Phi\right)=0
.

OPE formulae for
S_{1}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})

Since
z
and
\bar{z}
are treated independently, looking at the form of (
3.28
) we decompose
S^{+}_{1e}
inside a correlator as below:

\displaystyle S^{+}_{1e}(u,z,\bar{z})=j^{(+)}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})-2\bar{z}j^{(0)}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})+\bar{z}^{2}j^{(-)}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})

(B.7)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\partial}j^{a}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=-i\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\text{ }\theta(u-u_{p})\text{ }\left[\text{contact terms on $S^{2}$}\right]\,.

Like
S^{+}_{1}
, all three
j^{a}_{e}
have holomorphic weight
h=1
, so their Ward identities are practically the same as the holomorphic Kač-Moody current Ward identities in a usual 2D Euclidean CFT.

Finally, the general OPE in the
j\epsilon
-form of the
S^{+}_{1}
field was found in
[
46
]
to be:

\displaystyle\left\{S_{1}^{+}-({u-u_{p}})S^{+}_{0}\right\}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})

(B.8)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}\left(\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}^{L}\frac{\left(j^{(-)}_{n}\Phi\right)}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{n+1}}+\frac{\partial_{\bar{z}_{p}}\Phi}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}\right)+\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}^{M}\frac{\left(j^{(+)}_{n}\Phi\right)}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{n+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-2(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})\left(\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}^{N}\frac{\left(j^{(0)}_{n}\Phi\right)}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{n+1}}+\frac{\bar{h}_{p}\Phi}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}\right)\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})

\displaystyle\hskip 25.60747pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt\bar{\partial}^{3}S^{+}_{1}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0

(B.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.60747pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\left(\partial_{u}S^{+}_{1}-S^{+}_{0}\right)(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\,.

(B.10)

Again, the last two relations above are exact in the OPE limit. A conformal Carrollian primary multiplet
\Phi
must satisfy:

\displaystyle\left(j^{(-)}_{n}\Phi\right)=\left(j^{(0)}_{n}\Phi\right)=\left(j^{(+)}_{n-1}\Phi\right)=0\text{\hskip 14.22636pt for $n\geq 1$}

(B.11)

whereas for an
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
or Lorentz covariant quasi-primary, we only have
\left(j^{(+)}_{0}\Phi\right)=0
.

The corresponding correlators and OPEs involving the
S^{-}_{1}
field (replacing
S^{+}_{1}
) can be found by simply complex conjugating
(z\rightarrow\bar{z},\bar{h}\rightarrow h,\bm{\xi}\rightarrow\bm{\bar{\xi}})
the above mentioned equations. But it was shown in
[
46
]
, following
[
84
]
, that the fields
S^{+}_{0}
and
S^{-}_{1}
are not mutually local. This is so because while
\left(\bar{\partial}^{2}S^{+}_{0}\right)S^{-}_{1}\sim 0
respects (
B.4
),
S^{-}_{1}\bar{\partial}^{2}S^{+}_{0}
contains anti-meromorphic poles, thus violating the OPE commutativity property. In contrast, the OPE of
S^{+}_{0}
with
S^{+}_{1}
does not suffer from this problem; hence,
S^{+}_{0}
and
S^{+}_{1}
can be simultaneously treated as local fields.

Appendix C
Holomorphic superrotations generator

An alternative way to convert the
S^{2}
contact terms in the Ward identity (
3.25
) (or, its conjugate version)
into pole singularities is to extract from the RHS a
\bar{\partial}
(or,
\partial
) -derivative. By inverting these derivatives, the conformal Carrollian fields
T
and
\bar{T}
are defined as:

\displaystyle T(u,z,\bar{z})=\int\limits_{S^{2}}d^{2}{r}^{\prime}\frac{T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}(u,\vec{x}^{\prime})}{z-z^{\prime}}+\pi\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}T^{\bar{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}(u^{\prime},\vec{x})\hskip 7.11317pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 7.11317pt2\bar{\partial}T=\partial^{3}S^{-}_{1}\,,

(C.1)

\displaystyle\bar{T}(u,z,\bar{z})=\int\limits_{S^{2}}d^{2}{r}^{\prime}\frac{T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}(u,\vec{x}^{\prime})}{\bar{z}-\bar{z}^{\prime}}+\pi\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}T^{{z}}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}(u^{\prime},\vec{x})\hskip 7.11317pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 7.11317pt2{\partial}\bar{T}=\bar{\partial}^{3}S^{+}_{1}\,.

(C.2)

The fields
T
and
\bar{T}
respectively have dimensions
(\Delta,s)=(2,\pm 2)
. Since
S^{\pm}_{1}
have
(\Delta,s)=(0,\pm 2)
, the above relations directly imply that
(S^{+}_{1},\bar{T})
and
(S^{-}_{1},T)
are two shadow pairs (on
S^{2}
). Hence, if we choose
S^{+}_{1}
as a local field,
\bar{T}
must be treated as its non-local shadow.

With a string
X
of mutually local primaries, the
T
Ward identity was found, up to temporal contact terms, from the superrotation Ward identity to be
[
46
]
:

\displaystyle i\langle T(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\text{ }\theta(u-u_{p})\left[\frac{h_{p}}{(z-z_{p})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{z_{p}}}{z-z_{p}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 130.88268pt-\frac{u-u_{p}}{2}\left\{\frac{\partial_{u_{p}}}{(z-z_{p})^{2}}+\frac{2\bm{\xi}_{p}}{{(z-z_{p})}^{3}}+\pi\bar{\bm{\xi}}_{p}\partial\delta^{2}(\vec{x}-\vec{x}_{p})\right\}\right]\langle X\rangle

(C.3)

\displaystyle\text{with\hskip 7.11317pt}\langle\bar{\partial}T(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=-i\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\theta(u-u_{p})\left[\text{contact terms on $S^{2}$}\right]

\displaystyle\text{and\hskip 7.11317pt}\partial_{u}\langle T(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle\partial\bar{\partial}S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=[\text{temporal contact terms}]\,.

The last relation inspires the following decomposition of the
T
field inside the correlator
[
46
]
:

\displaystyle T(u,z,\bar{z})=\frac{u}{2}\partial\bar{\partial}S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})+T_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})

(C.4)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 7.11317pt

\displaystyle{\partial}_{u}\langle T_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=[\text{temporal contact terms}]

where the object (not a Carrollian field)
T_{e}
contains the modes generating the holomorphic superrotations:

\displaystyle z\rightarrow z^{\prime}=\mathcal{Y}(z)\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317pt\bar{z}\rightarrow\bar{z}^{\prime}=\bar{z}\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317ptu\rightarrow u^{\prime}=u\left(\frac{d\mathcal{Y}}{dz}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(C.5)

with
\mathcal{Y}(z)
being a meromorphic function. The
\langle T_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
‘Ward identity’ is the 3D Carrollian CFT incarnation of the 2D celestial conformal stress tensor Ward identity
[
14
]
. Consistently,
T_{e}
is the
S^{2}
shadow transformation of
S^{-}_{1e}
, from whose ‘Ward identity’ we can obtain the subleading negative-helicity energetically soft graviton theorem
[
12
,
13
]
.

We note down the generic OPE involving the
T
field in the
j\epsilon
-form
[
46
]
below:

\displaystyle\left(T-\frac{u-u_{p}}{2}\text{ }\partial\bar{\partial}S^{+}_{0}\right)(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\sum\limits_{n\geq 1}^{P}\frac{\left(L_{n}\Phi\right)}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{n+2}}+\frac{h_{p}\Phi}{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{z_{p}}\Phi}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})

(C.6)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}T(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\left(\partial_{u}T-\frac{1}{2}\partial\bar{\partial}S^{+}_{0}\right)(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 28.45274pt\text{(OPE exact)}\,.

(C.7)

For a conformal Carrollian primary,

\displaystyle\left(L_{n}\Phi\right)=0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for $n\geq 1$}\,,

(C.8)

whereas an
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
Lorentz quasi-primary needs to satisfy only
\left(L_{1}\Phi\right)=0
.

Without referring to the transformation properties of the conformal Carrollian primaries at all, we shall define the field (multiplet)
\Phi
as a primary under the symmetry-algebra (
3.38
) if it satisfies (
B.6
), (
B.11
), and (
C.8
), or equivalently, transforms as (
A.7
) under the wedge supertranslations
\mathcal{T}_{\wedge}(z,\bar{z})
with
\bar{\partial}^{2}\mathcal{T}_{\wedge}=0
, as (
A.8
) under the holomorphic superrotations
\mathcal{Y}(z)
, and as (
A.30
) under the wedge diffeomorphisms
\bar{V}(z,\bar{z})
with
\bar{\partial}^{3}\bar{V}=0
.

It is worth mentioning that the globally defined (on
S^{2}
) subset of the transformations (
3.33
), (
3.35
), and (
C.5
) consists of the ten
\text{ISL}(2,\mathbb{C})
Poincaré transformations. The above-described extraction of the various powers of
\partial
or
\bar{\partial}
from the 3D Carrollian CFT stress tensor Ward identities is precisely the minimum amount of extraction needed to construct the Carrollian fields that collectively contain all the Poincaré generators. In fact, we have not extracted a
\bar{\partial}
from the
T
Ward identity (
C.3
) even though it has a contact term (on
S^{2}
) singularity, since the field
T
already contains the modes generating the three holomorphic Lorentz transformations. Furthermore, as explained in
[
46
]
, we can treat the three Carrollian fields
S^{+}_{0}
,
S^{+}_{1}
, and
T
as mutually local,
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
quasi-primary fields (descending from the Identity field).

Appendix D
Central charges

In this appendix, we shall obtain the central charges of the 3D conformal Carrollian field theory by calculating the two-point correlators between the stress-tensor components, using the following correspondences:

\displaystyle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptu}(u,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=-2\bar{\partial}^{2}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

(D.1)

\displaystyle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\bar{z}}(u,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=-\bar{\partial}^{3}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

(D.2)

\displaystyle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt{z}}(u,z,\bar{z})

\displaystyle=-{\partial}^{3}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}\left[C_{\bar{z}\bar{z}}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+D_{\bar{z}\bar{z}}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})\right]

(D.3)

One of the crucial ingredients is the bulk null momentum space mode expansions (
3.40
) so that we have:

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left[C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})+D_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=-\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}{d\omega}\text{ }\omega\left[e^{-i\omega u}\left\{a_{+}^{\text{out}}-a_{+}^{\text{in}}\right\}+e^{i\omega u}\left\{{a_{-}^{\text{out}}}^{\dagger}-{a_{-}^{\text{in}}}^{\dagger}\right\}\right](\omega,z,\bar{z})

(D.4)

while the other being the graviton mode commutation relation given below
[
45
]
:

\displaystyle\left[a_{\alpha}^{\text{out}}\left(\omega_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1}\right),{a_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{\text{out}}}^{\dagger}\left(\omega_{2},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2}\right)\right]=\frac{16\pi^{3}}{\omega_{1}}\delta\left(\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}\right)\delta^{(2)}\left(z_{1}-z_{2}\right)\delta_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}

(D.5)

and similarly, for the incoming modes. We also recall that the operators
a_{\alpha}^{\text{out}}
and
a_{\alpha}^{\text{in}}
are related via the unitary
S
-operator as below
[
122
]
:

\displaystyle a_{\alpha}^{\text{out}}=S^{\dagger}a_{\alpha}^{\text{in}}S\hskip 14.22636pt\text{with}\hskip 14.22636pta_{\alpha}^{\text{in}}\equiv a_{\alpha}

(D.6)

and that the vacuum has the following properties:

\displaystyle S|0\rangle=|0\rangle\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptSa_{\alpha}^{\dagger}|0\rangle=a_{\alpha}^{\dagger}|0\rangle

(D.7)

By virtue of (
D.5
)-(
D.7
), we then have:

\displaystyle\left\langle 0\left|\partial_{u_{1}}\left[C_{zz}(u_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})+D_{zz}(u_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\right]\partial_{u_{2}}\left[C_{zz}(u_{2},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})+D_{zz}(u_{2},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})\right]\right|0\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}}{4\pi^{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\text{ }\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\left\langle 0\left|\left[e^{-i\omega_{1}u_{1}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{+}S-a_{+}\right\}+e^{i\omega_{1}u_{1}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{-}^{\dagger}S-a_{-}^{\dagger}\right\}\right](\omega_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left[e^{-i\omega_{2}u_{2}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{+}S-a_{+}\right\}+e^{i\omega_{2}u_{2}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{-}^{\dagger}S-a_{-}^{\dagger}\right\}\right](\omega_{2},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})\right|0\right\rangle\text{ }=\text{ }0

which implies that the each of the two-point correlators
\left\langle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\mu}\left(\mathbf{x}_{1}\right)T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\nu}\left(\mathbf{x}_{2}\right)\right\rangle
with
\mu,\nu\in\left\{u,\bar{z}\right\}
vanishes. The
\left\langle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}\left(\mathbf{x}_{1}\right)T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}\left(\mathbf{x}_{2}\right)\right\rangle
correlator is similarly
0
. The above calculation hence confirms that all the
\left\langle S^{+}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x}_{1}\right)S^{+}_{l}\left(\mathbf{x}_{2}\right)\right\rangle
and the
\left\langle T\left(\mathbf{x}_{1}\right)T\left(\mathbf{x}_{2}\right)\right\rangle
correlators vanish.

The following contribution is also zero similarly:

\displaystyle\left\langle 0\left|\partial_{u_{1}}\left[C_{zz}(u_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})+D_{zz}(u_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\right]\partial_{u_{2}}\left[C_{\bar{z}\bar{z}}(u_{2},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})+D_{\bar{z}\bar{z}}(u_{2},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})\right]\right|0\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}}{4\pi^{2}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\text{ }\omega_{1}\omega_{2}\left\langle 0\left|\left[e^{-i\omega_{1}u_{1}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{+}S-a_{+}\right\}+e^{i\omega_{1}u_{1}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{-}^{\dagger}S-a_{-}^{\dagger}\right\}\right](\omega_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left[e^{-i\omega_{2}u_{2}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{-}S-a_{-}\right\}+e^{i\omega_{2}u_{2}}\left\{S^{\dagger}a_{+}^{\dagger}S-a_{+}^{\dagger}\right\}\right](\omega_{2},z_{2},\bar{z}_{2})\right|0\right\rangle=0

which makes the
\left\langle T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679pt\mu}\left(\mathbf{x}_{1}\right)T^{u}_{\hskip 4.2679ptz}\left(\mathbf{x}_{2}\right)\right\rangle
correlators with
\mu\in\{u,\bar{z}\}
and each of the
\left\langle S^{+}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x}_{1}\right)T\left(\mathbf{x}_{2}\right)\right\rangle
correlators vanish.

Hence, we conclude that the above stress-tensor correlators contain no central charge.

Appendix E
Infinite tower of currents

We shall first sketch the outlines of the derivation, detailed in
[
48
]
, of the singular parts of the mutual OPEs between the fields
S^{+}_{2}
,
S^{+}_{0}
and
S^{+}_{1}
. The knowledge of the singular terms of the mutual OPEs between the latter two fields was crucial in this endeavour, that were completely determined in
[
46
]
, by demanding that the OPE commutativity property holds, after making the appropriate ansatz according to the general forms (
B.3
), and (
B.8
) and truncating those ansatz by the assumptions that:

1.

no time-independent local field in the theory possesses negative Carrollian scaling dimension
\Delta<0
; moreover, the time-independent local field with
\Delta=0=s
is uniquely the Identity operator,

2.

the fields
S^{+}_{0}
and
S^{+}_{1}
are Lorentz quasi-primaries (i.e.
a priori
covariant under
\text{SL}(2,\mathbb{C})
only).

The singular parts of the mutual OPEs (in the
j\epsilon
-form) between
S^{+}_{0}
and
S^{+}_{1}
were obtained as
[
46
]
:

\displaystyle S_{0}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 14.22636pt;\hskip 14.22636ptS_{0}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

\displaystyle S_{1}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\partial_{\bar{z}_{p}}S^{+}_{0}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{0}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

(E.1)

\displaystyle\left\{S_{1}^{+}-(u-u_{p})S^{+}_{0}\right\}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\partial_{\bar{z}_{p}}S^{+}_{1}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }2S^{+}_{1}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

Then the general form of the
S^{+}_{0}
OPE (
B.3
), the relation (
4.1
), the
S^{+}_{0}S^{+}_{1}
OPE, and the first assumption together completely fix the singular part of the
S^{+}_{0}S^{+}_{2}
OPE to be
[
48
]
:

\displaystyle S_{0}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

(E.2)

The singular terms of the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{0}
OPE can be readily obtained from the above using the OPE (bosonic) commutativity property, and the restrictions (
B.4
), (
B.5
), (
B.9
), and (
B.10
). Similarly, starting from an ansatz respecting the general form of the
S^{+}_{1}
OPE (
B.8
), the singular structure of the
S^{+}_{1}S^{+}_{2}
OPE was found to be:

\displaystyle S_{1}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{2}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }3S^{+}_{2}+(u-u_{p})\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{1}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

(E.3)

The
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{1}
OPE can then be deduced using the OPE commutativity property with the following restriction on
S^{+}_{2}
, analogous to (
B.4
) and (
B.9
), in mind:

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}^{4}S^{+}_{2}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 42.67912pt\text{(OPE exact)}

(E.4)

which was inferred in
[
48
]
by observing from (
E.2
) that:

\displaystyle S_{0}^{+}\bar{\partial}^{3+N}S^{+}_{2}\sim 0\hskip 7.11317pt{(N\in\mathbb{N})}\hskip 14.22636pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt\left(\bar{\partial}^{3+N}S^{+}_{2}\right)S^{+}_{0}\sim 0\hskip 28.45274pt\text{(OPE exact)}

and then demanding that the conformal Carrollian OPEs must be associative, just as in a 2D Euclidean CFT
[
123
]
. The condition (
E.4
) readily implies the following decomposition of the object
S^{+}_{2e}
(see (
4.2
)) inside a correlator:

\displaystyle S^{+}_{2e}(u,z,\bar{z})=-k^{(+2)}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})+3\bar{z}k^{(+1)}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})-3\bar{z}^{2}k^{(0)}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})+\bar{z}^{3}k^{(-1)}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})

(E.5)

such that

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\partial}k^{a}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=-i\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\text{ }\theta(u-u_{p})\text{ }\left[\text{contact terms on $S^{2}$}\right]

just analogous to (
B.1
) and (
B.7
). The holomorphic weight of the field
S^{+}_{2}
as well as all the four
k^{a}_{e}
is
h=\frac{1}{2}
. For future reference, we note down the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{1}
OPE below:

\displaystyle S_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})

\displaystyle\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{3}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}^{2}_{p}S^{+}_{2}+\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }2\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{2}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }3S^{+}_{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(u-u_{p})\left\{\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{1}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }2S^{+}_{1}\right\}+\frac{1}{2}(u-u_{p})^{2}\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{0}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

(E.6)

To find the (singular part of the)
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{2}
OPE, we first make the following ansatz consistent with the decomposition (
4.2
) and the restriction (
E.5
)
[
48
]
:

\displaystyle S_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{r=0}^{3}\sum_{s\geq 1}\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{r}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{s}}\text{ }A_{r,s}+(u-u_{p})\left\{\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{2}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }3S^{+}_{2}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 102.42992pt+\frac{1}{2}(u-u_{p})^{2}\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{1}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})

(E.7)

with
A_{r,s}(\mathbf{x}_{p})
being some yet-to-be-determined fields that are mutually local with
S^{+}_{2},S^{+}_{1},S^{+}_{0}
. Now, the singular part of the
S_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\partial_{u_{p}}S^{+}_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{p})
OPE obtained from this ansatz must be in complete agreement with that of the
S_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})
OPE (
E.6
) because of the relation (
4.1
). The non-trivial constraints, exact in the OPE limit, coming entirely from the
\mathcal{O}\left((u-u_{p})^{0}\right)
terms are listed below (where
\dot{}
represents time-derivative):

\displaystyle\dot{A}_{r,s\geq 2}\sim 0\hskip 14.22636pt,\hskip 14.22636pt\dot{A}_{0,1}\sim 0

\displaystyle\dot{A}_{3,1}\sim\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}^{2}_{p}S^{+}_{2}\hskip 14.22636pt,\hskip 14.22636pt\dot{A}_{2,1}\sim 3\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{2}\hskip 14.22636pt,\hskip 14.22636pt\dot{A}_{1,1}\sim 6S^{+}_{2}\,.

(E.8)

Thus all the local fields
{A}_{r,s\geq 2}
and
{A}_{0,1}
are time-independent (in the OPE limit) and have
\Delta<0
; all of them are hence set to zero by the first assumption. More interestingly, the constraints (
E.8
) imply that, for the consistency of the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{2}
OPE, there must
automatically
exist a local field
S^{+}_{3}
in the 3D Carrollian CFT, that, in the OPE limit, obeys
[
48
]
:

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{u}S^{+}_{3}-S^{+}_{2}\right)(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\,.

(E.9)

The dimensions of such a field
S^{+}_{3}
are
(\Delta,s)=(-2,2)
. Consistently with (
4.2
), the
S^{+}_{3}
field can be decomposed inside a correlator as:

\displaystyle S^{+}_{3}(u,z,\bar{z})=S^{+}_{3e}(u,z,\bar{z})+uS^{+}_{2e}(u,z,\bar{z})+\frac{u^{2}}{2}S^{+}_{1e}(u,z,\bar{z})+\frac{u^{3}}{3!}S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})

(E.10)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle{\partial_{u}}\langle S^{+}_{3e}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=\left[\text{temporal contact terms}\right]\,.

The automatic presence of the local field
S^{+}_{3}
may be interpreted as the non-closure of the algebra of the modes contained in the object
S^{+}_{2e}
. Finally, the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{2}
OPE is written down below:

\displaystyle S_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{p})

\displaystyle\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{3}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}^{2}_{p}S^{+}_{3}+\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }3\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{3}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }6S^{+}_{3}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(u-u_{p})\left\{\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{2}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }3S^{+}_{2}\right\}+\frac{1}{2}(u-u_{p})^{2}\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{1}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

(E.11)

Again, from the
S^{+}_{0}S^{+}_{3}
OPE together with the restriction (
E.4
) (and OPE associativity), it was shown in
[
48
]
that:

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}^{5}S^{+}_{3}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 42.67912pt\text{(OPE exact)}

(E.12)

revealing that an
S^{+}_{3}\Phi
OPE must be a quartic polynomial in
(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})
. Hence, analogous to (
E.5
),
S^{+}_{3e}
can be decomposed into five objects
l^{a}_{e}(u,z,\bar{z})
whose correlators are both holomorphic and time-independent in the OPE limit. The
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{3}
OPE was derived in
[
48
]
using the knowledge of the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{2}
OPE in exactly the same way (as reviewed above) the latter was obtained. The derivation revealed that for the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{3}
OPE to be consistent, a local field
S^{+}_{4}
with dimensions
(\Delta,s)=(-3,2)
must
automatically
be present in the theory, that satisfies:

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{u}S^{+}_{4}-S^{+}_{3}\right)(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 42.67912pt\text{(OPE exact)}

(E.13)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle\text{ }\bar{\partial}^{6}S^{+}_{4}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\,.

Recognising the above pattern, the
automatic
existence of a tower of mutually local Carrollian fields
S^{+}_{k+1}
(
k\geq 2
) satisfying the following conditions, just to render the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{k}
OPEs consistent, was recursively realised in
[
48
]
:

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}S^{+}_{k+1}-S^{+}_{k}\right)(t,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 14.22636pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 14.22636pt\bar{\partial}^{k+2}S^{+}_{k}(t,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim 0\hskip 28.45274pt\text{(OPE exact)}\,.

It is to be reemphasized that we only needed to postulate the presence of a field
S^{+}_{2}
obeying (
4.1
) in the 3D CarrCFT to begin with. Recursing the steps to derive the
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{2}
OPE (
E.7
), the general
S^{+}_{2}S^{+}_{k}
OPE was obtained in
[
48
]
as:

\displaystyle iS_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})S^{+}_{k}(\mathbf{x}_{p})

(E.14)

\displaystyle\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{3}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{2}S^{+}_{k+1}+\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}(k+1)\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{k+1}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2}(k+1)(k+2)S^{+}_{k+1}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+(u-u_{p})\left\{\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}S^{+}_{k}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }(k+1)S^{+}_{k}\right\}+\frac{1}{2}(u-u_{p})^{2}\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }S^{+}_{k-1}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

Appendix F
OPE formulae for
S_{2}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})
and subsubleading soft theorem

We first discuss how the Ward identity of the
S^{+}_{2}
field encodes the positive helicity subsubleading energetically soft graviton theorem
[
12
]
. The starting point in
[
48
]
was to postulate that the field
S^{+}_{2}
with a conformal Carrollian primary
\Phi
of a certain type, with
\left(\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot\Phi\right)=0=\left(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi\right)
and dimensions
(h,\bar{h})
, has the following OPE:

\displaystyle S_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})

\displaystyle\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{3}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{2}\Phi_{1}-\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }2\bar{h}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi_{1}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2}(2\bar{h})(2\bar{h}-1)\Phi_{1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(u-u_{p})\left\{\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }2\bar{h}\Phi\right\}+\frac{1}{2}(u-u_{p})^{2}\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\dot{\Phi}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})

(F.1)

with the unique local Carrollian field
\Phi_{1}
exactly satisfying
\dot{\Phi}_{1}\sim\Phi
in the OPE limit. This was motivated
17
17

17

Unlike its celestial counterpart (see however
[
124
]
), the conformal Carrollian weight
\Delta
is expected to be discrete. The reason is that a 3D Carrollian CFT primary corresponding to a 4D bulk mass-less scattering field must possess
\Delta=1
, and hence, all its descendants have integer Carrollian weights.
by the OPEs (

) which are valid for an infinite number of conformal Carrollian primaries
S^{+}_{k}
, each having
\left(\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot S^{+}_{k}\right)=0=\left(\bm{\xi}\cdot S^{+}_{k}\right)
and dimensions
(h,\bar{h})=\left(\frac{3-k}{2},-\frac{k+1}{2}\right)
. The OPE of the field
\Phi
may not completely fix that of the field
\Phi_{1}
; rather, it may happen that:

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(u,z,\bar{z})\sim\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }\Phi(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})+\text{(terms with temporal step-function factor)}

as are the cases for the fields
S^{+}_{k}
. We shall end this appendix with a
\text{Witt}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\displaystyle\displaystyle 3
\roundcap\put(0.5,0.5){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\raisebox{0.83333pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}

symmetry-based partial derivation of the above OPE.

Decomposing the
S^{+}_{2}
field according to (
4.5
) and (
4.6

), and applying the 3D Carrollian CFT OPE
\longleftrightarrow

[

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi\right]=3{z}_{p}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{2}\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{-\frac{1}{2};n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi\right]={z}_{p}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\left[3\bar{z}_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{2}\Phi_{1}+4\bar{h}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi_{1}+2u_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

(F.2)

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{\frac{1}{2};n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi\right]={z}_{p}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\left[3\bar{z}^{2}_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{2}\Phi_{1}+8\bar{h}\bar{z}_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi_{1}+2\bar{h}(2\bar{h}-1)\Phi_{1}+4\bar{z}_{p}u_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi+4\bar{h}u_{p}\Phi+u_{p}^{2}\dot{\Phi}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{\frac{3}{2};n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi\right]=3{z}_{p}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\left[\bar{z}^{3}_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{2}\Phi_{1}+4\bar{h}\bar{z}^{2}_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi_{1}+2\bar{h}(2\bar{h}-1)\bar{z}_{p}\Phi_{1}+2\bar{z}^{2}_{p}u_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi+4\bar{h}u_{p}\bar{z}_{p}\Phi+u_{p}^{2}\bar{z}_{p}\dot{\Phi}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

with the four modes
H^{2}_{a;\frac{1}{2}}
generating four global transformations. Thus, the 3D Carrollian CFT correlators must be invariant under these four global transformations, besides the ten Poincaré ones, if the field
S^{+}_{2}
exists in the theory. Interestingly, the above transformations generated by
S^{+}_{2}
are non-local in time (but local in space) since they act on the primary field with time-integration.

Demanding that the
\langle S_{2}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
correlator, where

is a string of the above-mentioned special Carrollian CFT primaries all with
\left(\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot\Phi\right)=0=\left(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi\right)

(especially at

) and recalling that the field

, the

Ward identity corresponding to the OPE (

) is uniquely given by (up to temporal contact terms):

\displaystyle i\langle S_{2}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=\sum\limits_{p=1}^{n}\theta

\displaystyle(u-u_{p})\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{3}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{2}\partial_{u_{p}}^{-1}-\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }2\bar{h}_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}\partial_{u_{p}}^{-1}+\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\bar{h}_{p}(2\bar{h}_{p}-1)\partial_{u_{p}}^{-1}\right.

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left.(u-u_{p})\left\{\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{z-{z}_{p}}\bar{\partial}_{p}-2\bar{h}_{p}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{z-{z}_{p}}\right\}+\frac{(u-u_{p})^{2}}{2}\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{z-{z}_{p}}\partial_{u_{p}}\right]\langle X\rangle\,.

(F.3)

With the anti-derivative operator
\partial_{u_{p}}^{-1}
changing
(\Delta,s)\rightarrow(\Delta-1,s)
, the
\langle S_{2e}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle

[

. Consequently, the
\langle S_{2e}^{+}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
‘Ward identity’ in the explicit

limit, with each primary in

having

, is equivalent to the subsubleading energetically soft graviton theorem. The
S_{2}^{+}
Ward identity corresponding to the postulated
S^{+}_{2}\Phi
OPE (
F.1
) thus uniquely encodes the subsubleading soft graviton theorem as desired, providing an indirect
a posteriori
justification for the said postulate.

We now partially derive the (singular part of the)

), postulated in

]
, using a
\text{Witt}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.99997pt]{\raisebox{-0.83333pt}{\begin{picture}\displaystyle\displaystyle 3

symmetry argument. We begin with the following generalization of the ansatz (

) for a conformal Carrollian primary
\Phi

\displaystyle S_{2}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{d=0}^{3}\sum_{s\geq 1}\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{d}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}^{s}}\text{ }B_{d,s}+(u-u_{p})\left\{\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }2\bar{h}\Phi\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 102.42992pt+\frac{1}{2}(u-t_{p})^{2}\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\dot{\Phi}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})

with the goal to determine the local fields

\longleftrightarrow

[

]

(F.5)

with

. Now, the following part of the algebra (

\displaystyle i\left[H^{0}_{a;n}\text{ },\text{ }H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};m}\right]=\left[3a+\frac{3}{2}\right]H^{1}_{a-\frac{3}{2};n+m}\hskip 14.22636pt;\hskip 14.22636pti\left[H^{1}_{a;n}\text{ },\text{ }H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};m}\right]=\left[3a+3\right]H^{2}_{a-\frac{3}{2};n+m}

together with the commutator below:

\displaystyle i\left[L_{n}\text{ },\text{ }H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};m}\right]=\left[-\frac{n}{2}-m\right]H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};n+m}

suggests that it may be possible to impose some constraints on the local fields
B_{3,s}
using the Jacobi identity involving the primary field
\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})
, the mode

and one of the five modes

. Furthermore, we can completely fix the fields

for

in terms of the fields

and

,

We now consider the Jacobi identity involving
\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})
,

and one of

we extract the following constraints on

from the said Jacobi identities:

\displaystyle\left(P_{r,-1}B_{s}\right)=0\hskip 7.11317pt\text{ for }s\geq 0,r\geq 0\hskip 14.22636pt;\hskip 14.22636pt\left(P_{r,0}B_{s}\right)=0\hskip 7.11317pt\text{ for }s\geq 1,r\geq-1\,,

\displaystyle\left(P_{-1,0}B_{0}\right)=\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi\hskip 14.22636pt;\hskip 14.22636pt\left(P_{r,0}B_{0}\right)=0=\left(P_{r,-1}B_{0}\right)\hskip 7.11317pt\text{ for }r\geq 0\,.

(F.8)

Obeying these constraints, we can readily write the

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})B_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})\text{ }\frac{\partial_{u_{p}}B_{0}}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}-\frac{\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi}{{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

(F.9)

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})B_{s}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})\text{ }\frac{\partial_{u_{p}}B_{s}(\mathbf{x}_{p})}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{ for }s\geq 1\,.

Next, replacing
H^{0}_{\pm\frac{1}{2};n\geq-\frac{1}{2}}
in the above Jacobi identity first with
H^{1}_{-1;n\geq 0}
, then with

and finally with

, we similarly obtain the following constraints (cf. the OPEs (

) and (

)):

\displaystyle\left(j^{(-)}_{r}B_{s}\right)=0\hskip 4.2679pt;\hskip 4.2679pt\left(j^{(0)}_{r}B_{s}\right)=\frac{3}{2}B_{r+s}\hskip 4.2679pt;\hskip 4.2679pt\left(L_{r}B_{s}\right)=-\frac{1}{2}(r+2s+1)B_{r+s}\hskip 8.53581pt\text{all for }s\geq 0,r\geq 1

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\hskip 7.11317pt

\displaystyle\left(j^{(-)}_{s}B_{0}\right)=0\hskip 7.11317pt;\hskip 7.11317ptB_{s}=\frac{2}{3}\left(j^{(0)}_{s}B_{0}\right)=-\frac{2}{s+1}\left(L_{s}B_{0}\right)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{all for $s\geq 1$}

implying that once we get to know the
S^{+}_{1}B_{0}
or the

OPE, all the fields

can be directly read off from the coefficients of the singular terms in the said OPEs.

We observe that the simplest choice for the field
B_{0}(\mathbf{x}_{p})
such that the OPE (

) is satisfied is
\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}^{2}_{p}\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})
with

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})\text{ }\frac{\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}\,.

(F.11)

Clearly, the

) conform to this choice. Remaining compatible with the constraints
(

), we can further choose the field
\Phi_{1}
to be a conformal Carrollian primary satisfying the following OPEs:

\displaystyle S_{1}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi_{1}-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }(2\bar{h}-1)\Phi_{1}+(u-u_{p})\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }{\Phi}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

(F.12)

\displaystyle T(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{\left(h-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Phi_{1}}{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{z_{p}}\Phi_{1}}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}-\frac{u-u_{p}}{2}\frac{\Phi}{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})^{2}}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

(F.13)

rendering all the fields
B_{s\geq 1}
to be 0. With this simplest choice of the field
B_{0}
, the commutator (
F.5
) simply reduces to:

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]=3{z}_{p}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\partial}^{2}_{p}\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

(F.14)

With the explicit commutator (
F.14
) at hand, one can now obtain the
\left[H^{2}_{-\frac{1}{2};n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]
one, as mentioned earlier, from the Jacobi Identity involving

,

On the other hand, using the 3D Carrollian CFT OPE
\longleftrightarrow

), we get (for

):

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{-\frac{1}{2};n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]=2{z}_{p}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\left[u_{p}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi+\sum_{s=0}^{n-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\left(n+\frac{1}{2}-s\right)_{s}}{z_{p}^{s}\cdot s!}\left(3\bar{z}_{p}B_{3,s}-B_{2,s}\right)\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})

which, upon comparison with (
F.15
), implies that
B_{2,0}=-2\bar{h}\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi
and
B_{2,s\geq 1}=0
.

Having recognized the commutators (

) and (

) to be the first two of the transformations (

in the above-mentioned Jacobi identity. Following the derivation of the fields

, one can figure out that

and
