from the third commutator in (
F.2
) and finally, from the last one,
B_{0,s\geq 0}=0
. Substituting the expressions obtained above for the fields
B_{d,s}
back into the ansatz (
F.4
), we find the singular part of the
S^{+}_{2}\Phi
OPE to be exactly (
F.1
).

It is important to emphasize that we have not uniquely determined the
\left[H^{2}_{a;n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]
commutators to be (
F.2
) from the constraints (
F.8
) and (
F.10
) arising from the symmetry arguments. Rather, the commutators (
F.2
) follow from symmetry once we make the following simplest possible choice for the field
B_{3,0}
, compatible with the said constraints:
B_{3,0}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}^{2}\Phi_{1}
with
\Phi_{1}
being a Carrollian CFT primary satisfying the OPE (
F.11
). Thus, the transformation properties of the Carrollian CFT primary
\Phi
under the action of the modes of the ‘universal’ generator fields
S^{+}_{0},S^{+}_{1},T
can not uniquely fix the transformations induced by the modes
H^{2}_{a;n}
contained in the object
S^{+}_{2e}
. This is consistent with the fact that the field
S^{+}_{2}
generates four new global spacetime transformations independent of the ten Poincaré ones (induced by the three ‘universal’ generators).

As shown in
[
48
]
, the
\left\langle S^{+}_{2}X\right\rangle
Ward identity (
F.3
) corresponding to the
S^{+}_{2}\Phi
OPE (
F.1
) encodes the 4D bulk soft graviton theorem up to the subsubleading order when each of the Carrollian CFT primaries in
X
possesses
\left(\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}\cdot\Phi\right)=0=\left(\bm{\xi}\cdot\Phi\right)
and scaling dimension
\Delta=1
. But, since the
S^{+}_{2}\Phi
OPE (
F.1
) is not unique from the symmetry perspective, so is the corresponding Ward identity (
F.3
). This translates into the following additional criterion for a Carrollian CFT primary
\Phi
if it is to correspond to a 4D bulk massless scattering field: under the action of the global generator
H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};\frac{1}{2}}
, it must transform as:

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{-\frac{3}{2};\frac{1}{2}}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})\right]=3\bar{\partial}^{2}_{p}\Phi_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})

(F.16)

as seen from the commutator (
F.14
), with
\Phi_{1}
being a conformal Carrollian primary (with
\Delta=0
) satisfying the OPE (
F.11
). In other words, the choice
\frac{1}{2}\bar{\partial}^{2}\Phi_{1}
for the field
B_{3,0}
is fortunately both the simplest and the one relevant for flat space holography.

Once we have found out how the field
\Phi
transforms under the action of the modes
H^{2}_{a;n}
, be it (
F.2
) or any other possibilities respecting the constraints (
F.8
) and (
F.10
), we can recursively infer what transformations the modes
H^{k\geq 3}_{a;n}
induce on
\Phi
just by using the Jacobi identities involving
\Phi(\mathbf{x})
,
H^{2}_{a;n}
and
H^{k-1}_{b;m}
, starting with
k=3
, because of the following part of the algebra (
4.11
):

\displaystyle i\left[H^{2}_{a;n}\text{ },\text{ }H^{k-1}_{b;m}\right]=\left[ak-3b\right]H^{k}_{a+b;n+m}\,.

For example, if the
\left[H^{2}_{a;n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x})\right]
commutators are indeed given by (
F.2
), all the
\left[H^{k\geq 3}_{a;n}\text{ },\text{ }\Phi(\mathbf{x})\right]
ones can be shown to be arising from the
S^{+}_{k\geq 3}\Phi
OPEs (
4.12
). All the fields
\Phi_{r\geq 0}
(cf. (
4.13
)) in these OPEs are Carrollian CFT primaries satisfying the following OPEs:

\displaystyle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi_{r}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})\text{ }\frac{\Phi_{r-1}(\mathbf{x}_{p})}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}\,,

\displaystyle S_{1}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\Phi_{r}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p})^{2}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}\Phi_{r}-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }(2\bar{h}-r)\Phi_{r}+(u-u_{p})\text{ }\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})}}\text{ }{\Phi_{r-1}}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,,

\displaystyle T(u,z,\bar{z})\Phi_{r}(\mathbf{x}_{p})\sim\lim\limits_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}-i\left[\frac{\left(h-\frac{r}{2}\right)\Phi_{r}}{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{z_{p}}\Phi_{r}}{\Delta\tilde{z}_{p}}-\frac{u-u_{p}}{2}\frac{\Phi_{r-1}}{(\Delta\tilde{z}_{p})^{2}}\right](\mathbf{x}_{p})\,.

(F.17)

Similarly as the
S^{+}_{2}\Phi
OPE (
F.1
), the
S^{+}_{k\geq 3}\Phi
OPEs given by (
4.12
) are of the form relevant for the 3D Carrollian holographic description of the 4D bulk physics of massless scattering.

Appendix G
Verification from bulk amplitudes

In this Appendix, we illustrate the results obtained in the core of the paper by performing various consistency checks on massless scattering amplitudes.

G.1
Soft graviton theorems

The leading case

We shall now use the correspondence (
4.41
) or equivalently, the correspondence (
4.52
) to verify the
\langle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
Ward identity (
3.17
) at
u\rightarrow\infty
directly from the correspondence between the 3D Carrollian CFT position space correlators and the 4D bulk null-momentum space
\mathcal{S}
-matrices (
3.4
). Only in the Carrollian
u\rightarrow\infty
limit which translates into the energetically soft
\omega\rightarrow 0
limit, the Carrollian CFT correlators on both sides of (
3.17
) can simultaneously correspond to bulk massless
\mathcal{S}
-matrices. For a finite
u
(that implies finite
\omega
), however, energy conservation prevents both sides of (
3.17
) from simultaneously corresponding to an
\mathcal{S}
-matrix.

We consider the case where
X
only contains three Carrollian CFT primaries corresponding to one positive helicity graviton and two negative helicity gravitons (in the ‘all outgoing’ convention). Then the Carrollian CFT correlator in the LHS of (
3.17
) corresponds to the following bulk tree-level Einstein-Yang-Mills four-graviton (MHV) amplitude (with the
S^{+}_{0}
field corresponding to the graviton
1^{+2}
)
[
125
,
47
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{4}(1^{+2},2^{-2},3^{-2},4^{+2})=4\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{\omega_{2}\omega_{3}\omega_{4}}{\omega_{1}}\frac{z_{23}^{4}\bar{z}_{14}^{4}}{z_{12}\bar{z}_{12}z_{13}\bar{z}_{13}z_{14}\bar{z}_{14}}\delta^{(4)}(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}q_{i})

(G.1)

with the four-particle delta function simplified as (with
z=\frac{z_{12}z_{34}}{z_{13}z_{24}}
)
[
11
]
:

\displaystyle\delta^{(4)}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}q_{i}\right)=\frac{1}{4\omega_{4}}\text{ }

\displaystyle\delta\left(\omega_{1}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}\right)\text{ }\delta\left(\omega_{2}-\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{14}}{z_{23}\bar{z}_{12}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\text{ }

\displaystyle\delta\left(\omega_{3}+\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{24}\bar{z}_{14}}{z_{23}\bar{z}_{13}}\right)\text{ }\delta\left(z_{12}z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}\bar{z}_{13}-z_{24}z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}\bar{z}_{34}\right)\,.

(G.2)

Using the map between the 3D Carrollian CFT correlators and the 4D massless amplitudes (
3.4
) and the correspondence (
4.41
), the correlator
\langle{S}^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle
corresponding to the above amplitude is found to be (with
\mathcal{A}_{4}\left(1^{+2},2^{-2},3^{-2},4^{+2}\right)\equiv\mathcal{A}_{4}\left(\epsilon_{1}\omega_{1},\left\{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}\right\}\right)
with
p\in\{2,3,4\}
):

\displaystyle\langle{S}^{+}_{0}(u_{1},z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})X\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}}{2\pi}\ldots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{4}}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\sum\limits_{r=2}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}u_{i}}\left[e^{-i\omega_{1}u_{1}}\mathcal{A}_{4}\left(\omega_{1}+i\delta,\left\{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}\right\}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 213.39566pt\left.+\text{ }e^{i\omega_{1}u_{1}}\mathcal{A}_{4}\left(-\omega_{1}+i\delta,\left\{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}\right\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega_{1}}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{3}}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{4}}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\left[\Omega_{1}u_{1}+\sum\limits_{i=2}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}u_{i}\right]}\text{ }\mathcal{A}_{4}\left(\Omega_{1}+i\delta,\left\{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}\right\}\right)

(G.3)

which explicitly shows the equivalence between the correspondences (
4.41
) and (
4.52
). The
\Omega_{1}
-delta-function then needs to be analytically continued; this is found to be:

\displaystyle\delta\left(\Omega_{1}+\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}\right)=\lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left[\frac{1}{\Omega_{1}+\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}-i\mu}-\frac{1}{\Omega_{1}+\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}+i\mu}\right]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\Omega_{1}\rightarrow\left(\Omega_{1}+i\delta\right)}\lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left[\frac{1}{\Omega_{1}+i\delta+\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}-i\mu}-\frac{1}{\Omega_{1}+i\delta+\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}+i\mu}\right]

(G.4)

Now, as said before, we shall be imposing the limit
u_{1}\rightarrow\infty
. Let us then look into the
\Omega_{1}
-integral first (with
\alpha\equiv\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}\in\mathbb{R}
):

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{u_{1}\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega_{1}}{2\pi}\text{ }\frac{e^{-i\Omega_{1}u_{1}}}{\Omega_{1}+i\delta}\text{ }\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left[\frac{1}{\Omega_{1}+\alpha-i(\mu-\delta)}-\frac{1}{\Omega_{1}+\alpha+i(\mu+\delta)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim\limits_{u_{1}\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\left[\frac{e^{-\delta u_{1}}}{2\pi i}\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha-i\mu}-\frac{1}{\alpha+i\mu}\right\}+\frac{e^{-(\delta+\mu)u_{1}}}{2\pi i}\frac{e^{i\alpha u_{1}}}{\alpha+i\mu}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\mu\rightarrow 0^{+}}\frac{1}{2\pi i}\left\{\frac{1}{\alpha-i\mu}-\frac{1}{\alpha+i\mu}\right\}+0=\delta\left(\alpha\right)\,.

Similarly, it can be verified that the
\Omega_{1}
-integral yields a vanishing result when
u_{1}\rightarrow-\infty
, in agreement with the retarded initial condition (
3.11
).

Next, for the simplifications of the various delta-functions involving
z_{ij}
and
\bar{z}_{ij}
appearing in (
G.3
), we shall closely follow
[
11
]
. Assuming
z_{ij}\neq 0
and
\bar{z}_{12}\neq 0
, we first rewrite
\delta\left(\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}\right)
as:

\displaystyle\delta\left(\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}}{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}\right)=\text{sgn}\left(z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}z_{34}\right)\frac{z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}{\omega_{4}z_{34}}\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\,.

(G.5)

On the support of the delta function
\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)
, we then simplify the following factor appearing in (
G.2
) as:

\displaystyle\delta\left(z_{12}z_{34}\bar{z}_{24}\bar{z}_{13}-z_{24}z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}\bar{z}_{34}\right)=\delta\left(z_{24}z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}\bar{z}_{34}\right)=\frac{\text{sgn}\left(z_{24}z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}\right)}{z_{24}z_{13}\bar{z}_{12}}\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)\,.

(G.6)

We now perform the three remaining integrals in (
G.3
) using the amplitude (
G.1
) and the four-particle delta function (
G.2
) and the simplifications (
G.5
)-(
G.6
) to find the value of the LHS of (
3.17
)
\big|_{u\rightarrow\infty}
as:

\displaystyle\langle S^{+}_{0}(\infty,z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})X\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{3}}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{4}}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\sum\limits_{i=2}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}u_{i}}\text{ }\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{\omega_{2}\omega_{3}}{\omega_{4}}\frac{z_{23}^{4}\bar{z}_{14}}{z_{12}z_{13}z_{14}}\frac{\text{sgn}\left(z_{24}z_{34}\right)}{z_{24}z_{34}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\text{ }\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)\delta\left(\omega_{2}-\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{34}}{z_{23}}\right)\delta\left(\omega_{3}-\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{23}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{4}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\text{ }e^{-i\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\left[u_{4}+u_{2}\frac{z_{34}}{z_{23}}+u_{3}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{23}}\right]}\epsilon_{4}\omega_{4}\frac{z_{23}^{2}\bar{z}_{14}}{z_{12}z_{13}z_{14}}{\text{sgn}\left(z_{42}z_{34}\right)}\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\text{ }\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{34}}{z_{23}}\right)\Theta\left(\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{23}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\text{ }\frac{\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{34}}{z_{23}}\right)\Theta\left(\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{23}}\right)}{{\left[u_{4}z_{23}+u_{2}{z_{34}}+u_{3}{z_{42}}\right]}^{2}}\text{ }\frac{z_{23}^{4}\bar{z}_{14}}{z_{12}z_{13}z_{14}}\text{ }\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)\,.

(G.7)

To evaluate the RHS of (
3.17
), we first note down the following tree-level Einstein-Yang-Mills three-graviton (MHV) amplitude
[
9
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{3}(2^{-2},3^{-2},4^{+2})=4\frac{\omega_{2}^{2}\omega_{3}^{2}}{\omega_{4}^{2}}\frac{z_{23}^{6}}{z_{34}^{2}z_{42}^{2}}\delta^{(4)}(\sum_{i=2}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}q_{i})

(G.8)

with the three-particle delta function simplified below
[
11
]
:

\displaystyle\delta^{(4)}\left(\sum_{i=2}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}q_{i}\right)=\frac{\text{sgn}\left(z_{23}z_{24}\right)}{4\omega_{2}^{2}z_{24}z_{23}}\text{ }\delta\left(\omega_{3}-\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\omega_{2}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{34}}\right)\text{ }\delta\left(\omega_{4}-\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\omega_{2}\frac{z_{23}}{z_{34}}\right)\text{ }\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\,.

(G.9)

The corresponding Carrollian correlator
\langle X\rangle
is then obtained to be:

\displaystyle\langle X\rangle

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{3}}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{4}}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\sum\limits_{i=2}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}u_{i}}\text{ }\mathcal{A}_{3}(2^{-2},3^{-2},4^{+2})

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{2}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\text{ }e^{-i\epsilon_{2}\omega_{2}\left[u_{2}+u_{3}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{34}}+u_{4}\frac{z_{23}}{z_{34}}\right]}\text{ }\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)\frac{\text{sgn}\left(z_{23}z_{24}\right)}{z_{24}z_{23}}\frac{z_{23}^{4}}{z_{34}^{2}}\text{ }\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{23}}{z_{34}}\right)\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{34}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{i\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\text{ }\frac{\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{34}}{z_{23}}\right)\Theta\left(\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{23}}\right)}{{\left[u_{4}z_{23}+u_{2}{z_{34}}+u_{3}{z_{42}}\right]}}\text{ }\frac{z_{23}^{3}}{z_{24}z_{34}}\text{ }\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)\,.

(G.10)

Using this expression for
\langle X\rangle
, we now proceed to calculate the RHS of the Ward identity (
3.17
)
{}_{u_{1}\rightarrow\infty}
(with
\bm{\xi}_{p}=\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}_{p}=0
) to find:

\displaystyle-i\sum\limits_{p=2}^{4}\frac{\bar{z}_{1}-\bar{z}_{p}}{z_{1}-z_{p}}\partial_{u_{p}}\langle X\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\text{ }\frac{\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{34}}{z_{23}}\right)\Theta\left(\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{23}}\right)}{{\left[u_{4}z_{23}+u_{2}{z_{34}}+u_{3}{z_{42}}\right]}^{2}}\text{ }\frac{z_{23}^{3}\bar{z}_{14}}{z_{24}z_{34}}\left[\frac{z_{34}}{z_{12}}+\frac{z_{42}}{z_{13}}+\frac{z_{23}}{z_{14}}\right]\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\text{ }\frac{\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{34}}{z_{23}}\right)\Theta\left(\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{z_{42}}{z_{23}}\right)}{{\left[u_{4}z_{23}+u_{2}{z_{34}}+u_{3}{z_{42}}\right]}^{2}}\text{ }\frac{z_{23}^{4}\bar{z}_{14}}{z_{12}z_{13}z_{14}}\text{ }\delta\left(\bar{z}_{24}\right)\delta\left(\bar{z}_{34}\right)

which matches exactly with the LHS of (
3.17
)
{}_{u_{1}\rightarrow\infty}
given by (
G.7
).

We have similarly verified the
\langle S^{-}_{0}(u_{1}\rightarrow\infty,z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})X\rangle
Ward identity for the case where
X
only contains three Carrollian CFT primaries corresponding to two positive helicity gluons and one negative helicity gluon (and the
S^{-}_{0}
field of course corresponds to the graviton
1^{-2}
) in the ‘all outgoing’ convention. The four-point tree-level Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitude relevant for the LHS is
[
79
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{4}(1^{-2},2^{-1},3^{+1},4^{+1})=2\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{\omega_{4}^{2}}{\omega_{1}}\frac{z_{12}z_{14}}{z_{13}}\frac{\bar{z}_{34}^{3}\bar{z}_{42}}{\bar{z}_{12}\bar{z}_{13}\bar{z}_{14}\bar{z}_{23}}\delta^{(4)}(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}q_{i})

(G.11)

while the tree-level Einstein-Yang-Mills three-gluon (anti-MHV) amplitude corresponding to the Carrollian CFT correlator
\langle X\rangle
is
[
7
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{3}(2^{-1},3^{+1},4^{+1})=2\frac{\omega_{3}\omega_{4}}{\omega_{2}}\frac{\bar{z}_{34}^{3}}{\bar{z}_{23}\bar{z}_{42}}\delta^{(4)}(\sum_{i=2}^{4}\epsilon_{i}\omega_{i}q_{i})\,.

(G.12)

Both sides of the
\langle S^{-}_{0}(u_{1}\rightarrow\infty,z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})X\rangle
Ward identity are then found to be equal to:

\displaystyle\langle S^{-}_{0}(u_{1}\rightarrow\infty,z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})X\rangle

\displaystyle=-i\sum\limits_{p=2}^{4}\frac{z_{1}-z_{p}}{\bar{z}_{1}-\bar{z}_{p}}\partial_{u_{p}}\langle X\rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{2(2\pi)^{3}}\text{ }\frac{\Theta\left(\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{4}\frac{\bar{z}_{34}}{\bar{z}_{23}}\right)\Theta\left(\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{4}\frac{\bar{z}_{42}}{\bar{z}_{23}}\right)}{{\left[u_{4}\bar{z}_{23}+u_{2}{\bar{z}_{34}}+u_{3}{\bar{z}_{42}}\right]}}\text{ }\frac{z_{12}\bar{z}_{34}^{2}}{\bar{z}_{12}\bar{z}_{13}\bar{z}_{14}}\text{ }\delta\left({z}_{24}\right)\delta\left({z}_{34}\right)\,.

Intriguingly, while the correlator
\langle X\rangle
corresponding to the tree-level three-gluon Einstein-Yang-Mills amplitude is divergent, each one of the correlators
\partial_{u_{p}}\langle X\rangle
, and hence the RHS above, are convergent
[
47
]
.

Having checked the validity of the Ward identity (
3.17
)
u→∞
with the above explicit Einstein-Yang-Mills examples, we now provide a proof of the same using a general 4D massless tree-level amplitude irrespective of the bulk theory. The main ingredient is the tree-level universality of the leading
[
17
]
and the subleading soft graviton theorems
[
12
]
which (for positive helicity in the ‘outgoing’ convention) state that:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\Omega\rightarrow 0}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(h_{+2}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z}),\left\{\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)\right\}_{1\leq p\leq n}\right)=\lim\limits_{\Omega\rightarrow 0}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}-\left[\sum\limits_{q=1}^{n}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{q}}{z-z_{q}}\frac{\epsilon_{q}\omega_{q}}{\Omega+i\delta}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{n}\frac{{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{q}\right)}^{2}\bar{\partial}_{q}+\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{q}\right)\left(s_{q}+\omega_{q}\partial_{\omega_{q}}\right)}{z-z_{q}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)\right]\mathcal{A}_{n}\left(\left\{\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)\right\}_{1\leq p\leq n}\right)\,.

(G.13)

By virtue of the correspondence (
4.52
), and the map between the 3D Carrollian CFT correlators and the 4D massless amplitudes (
3.4
), the LHS of (
3.17
) reads:

\displaystyle\langle S^{+}_{0}(u,z,\bar{z})X\rangle=\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i

\displaystyle\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}}{2\pi}\ldots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{n}}{2\pi}\left[e^{-i\Omega u-i\sum\limits_{r=1}^{n}\epsilon_{r}\omega_{r}u_{r}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\text{ }\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(h_{+2}(\Omega+i\delta,z,\bar{z}),\left\{\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)\right\}_{1\leq p\leq n}\right)\right]\,.

(G.14)

Since the massless amplitude
\mathcal{A}_{n+1}
is expected to die off at
\left|\Omega\right|\rightarrow\infty
, the
\Omega
-integrals in the two terms at the RHS for the case when
u\rightarrow\infty
can be combined into a sum of counter-clockwise complex contour-integrals (over
\zeta
):

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\Omega u}\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\oint\limits_{-i\delta}\frac{d\zeta}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\zeta u}\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\zeta+i\delta\right)-\sum_{\Omega^{*}\neq 0}\text{ }\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i\oint\limits_{\Omega^{*}-i\delta}\frac{d\zeta}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\zeta u}\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\zeta+i\delta\right)

with
\Omega^{*}=\Omega^{*}\left(\{\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p}\},\left\{\left|z_{pq}\right|^{2}\right\},\left\{\left|z-z_{p}\right|^{2}\right\}\right)\in\mathbb{R}
. The singularity of
\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\zeta+i\delta\right)
at
\zeta=-i\delta
is the leading soft graviton (simple) pole while the
\zeta=\Omega^{*}-i\delta
(with
\Omega^{*}\neq 0
) poles may correspond to the multi-collinear singularities. Now, we have:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\oint\limits_{\Omega^{*}-i\delta}\frac{d\zeta}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\zeta u}\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\zeta+i\delta\right)\propto e^{-i\Omega^{*}u}

which at large
u
oscillates violently for
\Omega^{*}\neq 0
; upon performing the remaining
\left\{\omega_{p}\right\}
-integrals in (
G.14
), each of the
\Omega^{*}\neq 0
terms are expected to produce vanishing result by the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma. Hence, in the
u\rightarrow\infty
limit, we can safely ignore these
\Omega^{*}\neq 0
terms to write just:

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\Omega u}\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)=\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}-\oint\limits_{-i\delta}\frac{d\zeta}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\zeta u}\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\zeta+i\delta\right)\,.

(G.15)

Using this and the knowledge of the singular behavior of
\mathcal{A}_{n+1}\left(\zeta+i0^{+}\right)
near
\zeta=-i0^{+}
given by (
G.13
), we evaluate the
\Omega
-integral in (
G.14
)
u→∞
to obtain:

\displaystyle\langle S^{+}_{0}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})X\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{u\rightarrow\infty}\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}}{2\pi}\ldots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{n}}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\sum\limits_{r=1}^{n}\epsilon_{r}\omega_{r}u_{r}}\oint\limits_{-i\delta}\frac{id\zeta}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\zeta u}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\text{ }\sum\limits_{q=1}^{n}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{q}}{z-z_{q}}\frac{\epsilon_{q}\omega_{q}}{\zeta+i\delta}\mathcal{A}_{n}\left(\left\{\tilde{\Phi}_{s_{p}}\left(\epsilon_{p}\omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)\right\}_{1\leq p\leq n}\right)

\displaystyle=-\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}}{2\pi}\ldots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{n}}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\sum\limits_{r=1}^{n}\epsilon_{r}\omega_{r}u_{r}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{n}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{q}}{z-z_{q}}\text{ }{\epsilon_{q}\omega_{q}}\text{ }\mathcal{A}_{n}\left(\left\{\tilde{\Phi}_{p}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=-i\sum\limits_{q=1}^{n}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{q}}{z-z_{q}}\text{ }\partial_{u_{q}}\left[\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{1}}{2\pi}\ldots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega_{n}}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\sum\limits_{r=1}^{n}\epsilon_{r}\omega_{r}u_{r}}\text{ }\mathcal{A}_{n}\left(\left\{\tilde{\Phi}_{p}\right\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-i\sum\limits_{q=1}^{n}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{q}}{z-z_{q}}\text{ }\partial_{u_{q}}\langle X\rangle

(G.16)

which is exactly the Ward identity (
3.17
) at
u\rightarrow\infty
(with
\bm{\xi}_{p}=\bar{{\bm{\xi}}}_{p}=0
). Furthermore, the
\Omega
-integral in (
G.14
)

automatically vanishes, in consistency with the retarded initial condition.

\displaystyle\qquad\sim\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}-\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}{\Omega+i\delta}\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}\right)^{2}a_{+}\left(\Omega_{p}+\Omega,z_{p},\frac{\left(\Omega_{p}-i\delta\right)\bar{z}_{p}+\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)\bar{z}}{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}\right)\,.

One can safely use the said map on both sides of the collinear splitting (

) since, upon inserting the remaining operators, both sides can be separately interpreted as bulk

-matrices with conserved four-momentum.
It is important to note that, as a function of (complexified)

(but not at

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i^{k+l}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\frac{e^{-i\Omega u}}{\left(\Omega+i\delta\right)^{k}}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega_{p}}{2\pi}\frac{e^{-i\Omega_{p}u_{p}}}{\Omega_{p}^{l}}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\text{ }\frac{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}{\Omega+i\delta}\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\times\text{ }a_{+}\left(\Omega_{p}+\Omega,z_{p},\frac{(\Omega_{p}-i\delta)\bar{z}_{p}+(\Omega+i\delta)\bar{z}}{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i^{k}\partial_{u_{p}}^{-l}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\frac{e^{-i\Omega u}}{(\Omega+i\delta)^{k}}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega_{p}}{2\pi}{e^{-i\Omega_{p}u_{p}}}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\frac{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}{\Omega+i\delta}\left(\frac{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}{\Omega_{p}-i\delta}\right)^{2}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\Omega+i\delta}{\Omega_{p}+\Omega}\right)^{m}

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\times\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m}}{m!}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}^{m}a_{+}\left(\Omega_{p}+\Omega,z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}i^{k}\partial_{u_{p}}^{-l}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\frac{e^{-i\Omega\left(u-u_{p}\right)}}{(\Omega+i\delta)^{k}}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega_{p}}{2\pi}{e^{-i\Omega_{p}u_{p}}}\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}}{{z-z_{p}}}\frac{\Omega_{p}^{2}}{(\Omega+i\delta)\left(\Omega_{p}-\Omega-i\delta\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\Omega+i\delta}{\Omega_{p}}\right)^{m}\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m}}{m!}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}^{m}a_{+}\left(\Omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)\,.

(G.20)

The result of the
\Omega
-integral is given below (

\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\frac{e^{-i\Omega\left(u-u_{p}\right)}}{(\Omega+i\delta)^{k-m+1}\left(\Omega_{p}-\Omega-i\delta\right)}=\begin{cases}\theta\left(u-u_{p}\right)\sum\limits_{r=m}^{k}\frac{\left(u-u_{p}\right)^{k-r}}{i^{k-r+1}(k-r)!\text{ }\Omega_{p}^{r-m+1}}&,k\geq m\\
0&,k<m\end{cases}

where we have used the fact that the integrand in (

is not singular

at

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\times\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\Omega+i\delta}{\Omega_{p}}\right)^{m}\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m}}{m!}\bar{\partial}_{p}^{m}\text{ }a_{+}\left(\Omega_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)

Alternatively, one can use the series expansion (

{}_{2\bar{h}_{p}=-1}

For the special case

, (

) reduces to:

\displaystyle-i\theta\left(u-u_{p}\right)\sum_{r=0}^{k}\sum_{m=0}^{r}\frac{\left(\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{p}\right)^{m+1}}{\left(z-z_{p}\right)\cdot m!}\text{ }\frac{\left(u-u_{p}\right)^{k-r}}{(k-r)!}\text{ }\bar{\partial}_{p}^{m}\partial_{u_{p}}^{1-r}S^{+}_{0}\left(u_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p}\right)

\displaystyle{\Phi}(u)=i\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\text{ }e^{-i\Omega u}\text{ }a_{s}(\Omega)\hskip 5.69054pt\Longrightarrow\hskip 5.69054pt\Phi_{k}(u):=\partial_{u}^{-k}\Phi(u)=i^{k+1}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\Omega}{2\pi}\text{ }\frac{e^{-i\Omega u}}{\Omega^{k}}\text{ }a_{s}(\Omega)

\displaystyle ia_{s}(\Omega)=\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}{du}\text{ }e^{i\Omega u}\text{ }\Phi(u):=\tilde{\Phi}(\Omega)\hskip 28.45274pt(\text{for }\Omega\in\mathbb{R}-\{0\})

A comment regarding the similar derivation in
[
47
]
of the conformal Carrollian OPE starting from the collinear splitting (
4.45
) of one outgoing positive helicity graviton and another (physically) outgoing Einstein-Yang-Mills particle, and using the unilateral Fourier correspondence (
3.4
) between the Carrollian correlator/massless amplitude of
[
43
,
45
]
is in order. Though the coefficient of the most singular term in the Carrollian graviton OPE
C_{zz}(u,z,\bar{z})C_{zz}(u_{p},z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})
so obtained was found divergent, the descendant OPE
\partial_{u}C_{zz}(\mathbf{x})\partial_{u_{p}}C_{zz}(\mathbf{x}_{p})
turned out to be well-defined (finite)
[
47
]
. Defining the Carrollian fields
C^{+}_{k\geq 0}(u,z,\bar{z})
below, in analogy with the
S^{+}_{k}
fields (
4.28
):

\displaystyle C^{+}_{k}(u,z,\bar{z})=-\frac{2\pi}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }(u-u^{\prime})^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})

one however finds, using the said finite, descendant OPE, that all such
C^{+}_{k}(\mathbf{x})C^{+}_{l}(\mathbf{x}_{p})
OPEs at finite
u
diverge. The finiteness of the
S^{+}_{k}S^{+}_{l}
OPEs at finite
u
(
4.7
), in view of the correspondence (
4.28
), may then be attributed to a remarkable cancellation between the divergent pieces of the four OPEs:
C^{+}_{k}C^{+}_{l}
,
C^{+}_{k}D^{+}_{l}
,
D^{+}_{k}C^{+}_{l}
and
D^{+}_{k}D^{+}_{l}
.

On the contrary, all the
C^{+}_{k(e)}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})
‘OPEs’ were shown to be finite in
[
47
]
, with the object
C^{+}_{k(e)}
defined analogously to (
4.29
) as:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi}C^{+}_{k(e)}(u,z,\bar{z})=\frac{(-)^{k}}{k!}\int\limits_{-\infty}^{u}du^{\prime}\text{ }{u^{\prime}}^{k}\text{ }\partial_{u^{\prime}}C_{zz}(u^{\prime},z,\bar{z})

just as the
S^{+}_{k(e)}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})\Phi(\mathbf{x}_{p})
‘OPEs’ extracted from (
4.12
). Following the algorithm of
[
47
]
, it can also be shown that the
C^{+}_{1-k(e)}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})C^{+}_{1-l(e)}(u_{p}\rightarrow\infty,z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})
‘OPE’ is same (upto a constant factor) as the celestial conformally soft graviton OPE
H^{k}(z,\bar{z})H^{l}(z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})

[
22
]
encoding the
Lw_{1+\infty}
symmetry. This is to be contrasted with the conformal Carrollian OPE
S^{+}_{k}S^{+}_{l}
(
4.7
) at finite
u,u_{p}
(and also the
S^{+}_{k(e)}(u\rightarrow\infty,z,\bar{z})S^{+}_{l(e)}(u_{p}\rightarrow\infty,z_{p},\bar{z}_{p})
‘OPE’) containing exactly the same quantum symmetry algebra, that was derived intrinsically from Carrollian symmetry arguments in
[
48
]
or above using the bilateral Fourier correspondence (
4.53
) on the Einstein-Yang-Mills graviton collinear splitting (
G.19
).
